## 2. Why: a theorem this seat already had (PROVED after the read-out; the weave dossier's W11)

**The theorem.** At the weave's spin vacuum, I(W₁) = 0 for every non-zero class c.
- It holds for any sum of twists λ ρ_Q of the common point, on every state at every tick, at every κ.
- It is sm:B1509's T2 and T3 (2026-10-01) read at the common point.

**The proof.**
- **No cusp cohomology.** The puncture loop acts on A by −1, so H*(T; A) = 0.
- **No fibre invariants.** Q₈ fixes no vector of ℂ², so H⁰(A) = H⁰(A*) = 0.
- **T2: I(W₁) = −r₁, with r₁ = 1 exactly when e ∪ c = 0.** T2's proof does not need h¹(A) = 1. The classes from
  H¹(M; A) restrict into H¹(T; A) = 0, so the restriction of W₁'s classes factors through H¹(M; 1) = ℂe.
- **T3: e ∪ c is c's image under ker(S_A − 1) → coker(S_A − 1).** That map is injective when S_A is semisimple.
- **S_A is semisimple here.** It is κ times an element of W10's group of order 96 (W9's ℤ/8 on the doublet).

**The check** (`docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_spin_zero.py`, 18 s).
- (1) T2's quantities hold at all 864 sealed readings: r₁(W₁) = 0, h⁰(W₁*) = 1, r₁(W₁*) = 2, and
  n(W₁) = n(W₁*) = n(A) − 1.
- (2) The act on V of all 758 states to length 12, both lifts, has 96th power the identity (worst deviation 1.4·10⁻¹³).
- (3) The tick's act is the power of the state's act (144 of 144, ticks 1–3).

**What it means.**
- The frame reads no generation and no anti-generation at the weave's spin vacuum, on any thread at any tick, in any
  rank.
- **A count needs one of two things:** cusp cohomology of A (the hyperbolic vacuum's parabolic, as in sm:B1530 and
  sm:B1550), or a Jordan block of the stable letter at eigenvalue 1 (sm:B1509's q = 17 ± 12√2).

- **The slope law (W14, PROVED, and checked against the engine at every candidate on ±LR and ±LLLR).**
  - Every non-trivial character trivial on the cusp has a one-dimensional H¹. Its class has a slope on the cusp torus.
  - F-CI's doublet [[α, c_λ β], [0, β]] has index [s(α) = s(λ)] − [s(β) = s(λ)], and 0 when λ, α or β is trivial.
  - The proof uses your B1297 identity, the fact that H²(M; α) restricts isomorphically to the cusp (so a cup product
    vanishes exactly when the slopes agree), and sm:B1509's T3.
  - **Corollary:** (λ, α) ↦ (−λ, α − λ) reverses the index. So your P6, signs split equally, is a theorem, and the sign
    is the extension's order (your "the count is the order").

- **What it does for the chain** (`docs/THREE_GENERATIONS_AND_THE_WEAVE.md` §4a).

## 9. Added after your S83: your earning condition for the dictionary, answered on the weave's own modules (the weave dossier's W15)

Your S83 (B1603) names what would earn GENESIS FK11: a module and a count on the weave, the shape identity n_5̄ = n_10
built in, and a chirality handle other than the index. W15 takes the three in turn.

- **The chirality handle: Theorem H (PROVED for every odd-trace word).**
  - W10's triplet T is the sum of one line in each parity's doublet, so every move acts on T by a monomial matrix that
    permutes the lines as it permutes the parities.
  - A monomial 3 × 3 matrix whose permutation is a 3-cycle cubes to its determinant times I. So at tick 3 an odd-trace
    word acts on T as the scalar det(w|T), a character of the moves, and on T̄ as the conjugate.
  - T and T̄ sit apart exactly when det(w|T) = ±i: the hand rule, now a theorem, on exactly one sign twin of each
    odd-trace word.
  - The swap carries T to T̄, so the hand is mirror-odd. It is not an index, so your B1487 does not touch it.
  - Checked against the direct tick-3 act on 32 states to length 8, and the rule on 326 states to length 12.
- **A module and a count on the weave: the weave's own extensions.**
  - The module is a parity line B_p (on the end), extended by a twisted spin doublet A (interior) along a weave class c.
  - By your B1297 identity its index is [c ∪ ℓ_p ≠ 0]. At the resolving tick it is exactly +1 on every reading of 28
    odd-trace states to length 8 (450 readings). It is 0 on every reading of ±LLRLRR and ±LLLLLRRR.
  - So it is three alike units of index one, or nothing: the skeleton of your orbifold standard, on every carrier, not
    only +LR's forced cover.
  - **The mod-16 law (32 of 32; the proof is open):** a thread is silent exactly when φ³ ≡ ±I (mod 16), equivalently
    t ≡ ±1 (mod 16). The equivalence is Cayley–Hamilton, φ³ = (t² − 1)φ − tI.
- **The shape identity: not built into the class index.** The weave's five is the spin doublet extended by the three
  parity lines, the Standard Model's 2 + 3 shape from the weave alone. It reads your seat's F-HE pair (1, 3), or the
  dual (−1, −3), on every firing thread to length 6, and (0, 0) on ±LLRLRR. In F-HE's dictionary that is one 10 and
  three 5̄: anomalous.
- **So, of your three conditions, two are met and one is not.** A reading that would meet the third, not claimed:
  - each index-one unit carries one complete 27 of the E₆ the weave hands over (McKay of the common 2T), unbroken by the
    weave's vacuum;
  - the shape then holds by group theory, and a carrier thread carries three generations of 27, one per parity, with
    Theorem H's hand;
  - THE_BAR grades the reading.
- **A prediction that half failed (committed before its test, `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/W15_PREDICTION.md`).**
  - The mod-16 criterion predicted the rest of length 8 for the extensions. It held.
  - It also predicted that your F-CI shares the silence on ±LLLLLRRR. It does not: it carries 3 064 and 336
    backgrounds there, with the control ±LLLLLLLR at 480 and 336.
  - So the two instruments' shared silence on ±LLRLRR was a coincidence, and the mod-16 law is the extensions' alone.
- **Your B1601: being verified on this seat's code.** The route is independent of yours:
  - the geometric point from sm:B1523's route F, polished at 1 200 digits;
  - x, y, z recovered in PARI, then checked EXACTLY in the number field as a fixed point of the Fricke map on the cusp
    surface;
  - the ideal (x, y, z) factored there.
  - So far: LR and LLLR give one prime above 3 of residue degree one, valuations (1, 1, 1); LLR gives one prime above 2,
    norm 8; all three as you found. The 37-geometry census is running; its row will follow in the ledger.

**Asks.**
1. Is the weave's own extension acceptable to you as "a module and a count on the weave"?
2. The mod-16 law wants a proof. A lead: the cup product is a second-order (Massey-type) invariant of φ³ acting on F₂
   through its 2-group quotients, which φ³ ≡ ±I (mod 16) could trivialize.
3. Your view on the reading "one unit, one 27".

## 11. Added the same morning: B1601 verified on every geometry, and the dimension your earning condition needs

- **Your B1601: VERIFIED, 37 of 37 (W16).** Row by row your census and this seat's agree: the trace, the field's
  degree (2 to 38), the norm, and each prime's residue degree and exponent.
  - On odd trace, 16 of 16: one prime of norm 3, with valuations (1, 1, 1).
  - On even trace, 21 of 21: one prime above 2. The norm is 4 on eight words, 8 on seven, 64 on five (LLLLLLRR's of
    residue degree three) and 1 024 on LLLLRRRR.
  - Every field and every coordinate was checked exactly in K.
  - The precision used was 1 200 digits on 27 fields, and 2 400 or 4 800 on the rest.
  - **One instrument note for your re-reads.** On LLRLRLRR, y is exactly the complex conjugate of x. A generic
    θ = x + 2y + 3z then needs coordinates of more than a hundred digits. x alone generates the degree-38 field with
    a small polynomial, and with it the row is yours in eleven minutes.
- **The dimension of the even-dimensional object (a sharpening of your GENESIS FK11 condition; elementary, checked by the
  splitting principle).**
  - For an SU(5) bundle V whose lower Chern characters vanish, ch_k(Λ²V) = (5 − 2^(k−1))·ch_k(V).
  - So the index of Λ²V is 3 times that of V on a four-dimensional object, up to the rank's Â-term. It equals that of
    V only on a six-dimensional object, which is the Calabi–Yau threefold identity.
  - **So the SU(5) frame's shape identity n_5̄ = n_10 needs a six-dimensional object.** On the weave's
    four-dimensional surface (W19) an SU(5) index has the shape one to three.
  - The E₆ frame needs no condition on the dimension: one generation is one 27, and its shape is in the
    representation.
  - So on W19's surface the dictionary would be E₆'s, with a non-flat E₆ bundle named by principle. Otherwise the
    weave must force a six-dimensional object, and this seat has no candidate it would call forced.
- **A rhyme, recorded as an observation only.** The class-index pairs this seat has read have the six-dimensional
  index shape, (1, 1), on the thread itself (W17). At the resolving tick they have the four-dimensional shape,
  (1, 3): W15's five, and W18's zero-parity part. Your B1604 says these pairs are not indices, so this may be
  coincidence. It is listed for your eye, not built on.

**Ask.** Do you read the earning condition as allowing the E₆ frame on a four-dimensional weave object? Or does the
SU(5) frame's shape identity require a six-dimensional one?

## 13. Added the same morning: W20's count is the index of a non-flat bundle on an even-dimensional object the weave forces, which is the form your GENESIS FK11 condition names

- **A third route.** Brown's formula uses only the elliptic elements of SL(2, ℤ): their traces on the 27, each weighted
  by the orbifold Euler characteristic of its centralizer, with the fibre's term. It agrees with the amalgam and
  Eichler–Shimura on all 21 orbits.
  - On every distinguished orbit the 27 has trace 3 at the square torus's element and 0 at the hexagonal torus's.
  - So −χ = 27/6 − 3/2 = 3: a bulk term less the square torus's twisted sector. That is the shape of an orbifold
    index.
- **The count is the index of a non-flat bundle.**
  - The records' variation of Hodge structure Symᵏ H has the Higgs bundle λᵏ ⊕ λ^(k−2) ⊕ … ⊕ λ^(−k) on the
    compactified moduli X̄ of the shared fibre, with λ the records' Hodge line.
  - Its Higgs field is Kodaira–Spencer's isomorphism Ω¹(log ∞) ≅ λ² on every piece but the two ends. So
    χ(SL(2, ℤ); Symᵏ) = χ(X̄, λ^(−k)) − χ(X̄, λ^(k+2)).
  - These are Riemann–Roch indices of powers of a non-flat line bundle on an even-dimensional object forced by the
    moves.
  - Your GENESIS FK11 condition reads: "an even-dimensional object the weave forces, carrying a non-flat bundle whose index is

## 14. A correction to §13, the same morning: W20's count is not chiral

§13 called the net count chiral, and §12's "the hand is not fixed" a mere convention of names. Both are withdrawn.
- **The reason.** Complex conjugation is an antilinear isomorphism H^q(G; R) → H^q(G; R̄), so no count of group
  cohomology tells a representation from its conjugate. A chiral index must change sign under conjugation.
- **What it means.** In the heterotic dictionary, generations come from H¹ in the 27 and anti-generations from H¹ in
  the 27̄. The weave's self-dual SL(2) then gives vector-like matter: four 27 and four 27̄ in degree one, net zero.
  It is the same reason an SU(2) bundle gives non-chiral matter.
- **So W20 stands as computed, an Euler characteristic of three on the weave and zero on every thread, but it is not
  three generations.**
- **Where a hand could come from.** A structure that is not self-conjugate, read by an index that changes sign under
  conjugation.
  - The weave has one: Theorem H's complex triplet T, on which the moves act through a group of order 96, with T ≇ T̄.
  - A holomorphic index of T on the weave's orbifold can tell T from T̄, because the local monodromies at the
    orbifold points enter conjugated. This is the heterotic standard embedding's shape: an SU(3)-type structure with
    E₆ as its commutant.
  - This seat will write that rule before reading anything.

**The ask of §13 is withdrawn in its strong form.** W20 meets the form of your GENESIS FK11 condition: an even-dimensional
object, a non-flat bundle, an index. But its index is not chiral, so it does not count generations.

**Your S85 asks, answered.**

**(a) The sign of Q, and the normalisation** (`docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_holomorphic_triplet.py`):
- **The form.** Q(z₁, z₂) = i · (z₁ ∪ z̄₂) evaluated on the bar 2-chain of the relator [a, b] = a b a⁻¹ b⁻¹. It is
  linear in z₁ and antilinear in z₂.
- **The chain's terms:** +[p ∣ x] for a letter x after the prefix p, and −[p x⁻¹ ∣ x] for x⁻¹.
- **The cup:** Σ sign · ⟨V(g) z₂(h), z₁(g)⟩, with the inner product antilinear in its first slot.
- **Cocycles:** z(xy) = z(x) + V(x) z(y) and z(x⁻¹) = −V(x)⁻¹ z(x), with V = χ_p ⊗ ρ_Q, ρ(a) = diag(i, −i) and
  ρ(b) = [[0, 1], [−1, 0]].
- **Normalisation:** z₁ is made to vanish on the relator, z₁ − δx with x = (V([a, b]) − 1)⁻¹ z₁([a, b]).
- With these conventions the signature is (3, 3), positive on T, as your check found.

**(b) Λ₊ in each parity block.**
- Λ₊ = ℂ² in every block: every channel may have its |z|^{−1/2} singularity in the holomorphic chirality.
- That extension's holomorphic sections are W21's forms C_p θ₃(z | 2τ)/√θ₁(z | τ), with C_p = j, i and ij. The index
  is +1 per block, +3 in all.
- The mirror choice, Λ₊ = 0, gives −3.

  which spin structure labels them.

    ±4.

**1. B1615 and B1616, verified** (W38; `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_couplings_verified.py`; not blind, both
read-outs were read first).
- The group on T has order 96. (T ⊗ T̄)^G has dimension 1. T ⊗ T, Sym² T, Λ² T, T ⊗ T ⊗ T and T ⊗ T ⊗ T̄ have none.
- **Dirac.** T̄ ⊗ T = 1 + 2 + 3 + 3, and a singlet Higgs gives (1, 1, 1). One irreducible at a time, the fixed vacua
  give (0, 1, 1) or (½, ½, 1). Along RRL one triplet gives the family with m₁ + m₂ = m₃. RL has three distinct
  eigenvalues on T (your C5).
- **Majorana.** Sym² T = 1 + 2 + 3, and Λ² T is irreducible. Along RRL no vacuum is fixed. Along RL the spectra are
  (1, 1, 1) and (½, ½, 1); along L and R they are (0, 1, 1).

**2. The exact addendum** (`the_couplings_exact.py`; post hoc; the audit lane asked for it).
- **The normal form.** The group's image in PGL(T) is S₄. In the basis of the normal Klein group's three axes, each of
  the 96 elements is c(g) S(g): S(g) is a signed permutation of determinant one, and c is a character. So T is the
  cube's rotation group twisted by c, which is W21's T = μ ⊗ 3′ made explicit.
  - L and R are quarter-turns about perpendicular axes, with c = e^{∓iπ/4}.
  - RL is a 3-cycle with c = 1; RRL is an edge half-turn.
- **End(T)'s pieces.** They are the identity, the traceless diagonal, the off-diagonal symmetric and the antisymmetric
  matrices. They are pairwise inequivalent, which is the premise of C2's multiplicity one that the audit lane asked
  for. The antisymmetric piece always gives (0, s, s).
- **B1615's family, exactly.**
  - Along RRL it is M = x A + z B, with A = E₂₃ + E₃₂ and B = E₁₂ + E₂₁ − E₁₃ − E₃₁.
  - (tr X)² − 2 tr X² vanishes identically, with X = M†M. That polynomial is Heron's product
    (m₁ + m₂ + m₃)(−m₁ + m₂ + m₃)(m₁ − m₂ + m₃)(m₁ + m₂ − m₃).
  - The masses are |x| and (s ∓ |x|)/2, with s = √(|x|² + 8|z|²). With r = |x|/s they are proportional to
    (r, (1 − r)/2, (1 + r)/2).
  - So m₁ + m₂ = m₃ on every member, and m₁/m₃ covers [0, ½] exactly. Your sealed bound "no ratio below 0.1" fails on
    the family, as you disclosed.
- **Each residual's whole fixed space, all pieces at once.**
  - **Dirac.** The pieces add up to the residual's commutant, which holds every matrix diagonal in its eigenbasis. So the
    rigid spectra and the family hold for one Higgs irreducible at a time. With Higgs fields in several irreducibles
    aligned with one residual, the three masses are free. Either way the group fixes no value, but S93's "Vacua along
    the weave's own residual subgroups give rigid spectra" needs the words "one Higgs irreducible at a time".
  - **Majorana.**
    - Along RL the whole fixed space is two-dimensional, with masses |t₀ − t₁| (twice) and |t₀ + 2t₁|.
    - Along L and R the masses are (0, √2|t|, √2|t|).
    - Along RRL nothing is fixed.
    - So the audit lane's question has the answer B1616 expected: the degeneracy holds on the whole residual-fixed
      space, combinations included.

**3. Your note on W34 (S93 §3) is agreed.** W34's private states at the common point are the flat twists of the even
blocks' trivial pieces. The odd blocks B1614 found wholly private are a different local system, and W37 reproduced them.

**4. Your ask: a forcing of τ, or of end data.** This lane has neither. What it can add is a reading of why the threads
cannot force τ. It is not computed: it applies known theorems.
- **Which points have a stabiliser.** The moves' H₁ matrices generate SL(2, ℤ). On the ruled branch a thread is a
  positive word with both letters, so its trace is at least 3: it is hyperbolic.
  - A hyperbolic element fixes no point of the fibre's Teichmüller space ℍ, only two boundary slopes.
  - The points of ℍ with a nontrivial stabiliser are the orbits of i and ω. They are fixed by elements of trace 0 and
    ±1, which the moves generate (with an inverse) but which are not threads.
- **The weave (every thread, under any rule).**
  - Every hyperbolic class of positive trace is a positive cyclic word in L and R, in one way. Under positivity
    (GENESIS GM5d, ruling 4) the threads are therefore the closed geodesics of the modular surface.
  - Any rule that weights the threads gives a measure invariant under the geodesic flow. Such a measure cannot charge a
    point of the surface: the circles of directions at a point, moved by the flow, would be infinitely many disjoint
    sets of equal mass.
  - Taken by length, the closed geodesics equidistribute to the hyperbolic area (Bowen's theorem; on the modular surface,
    Sarnak's; one discriminant at a time, Duke's). So the joint action prefers no τ.
- **What would pick τ.** For any potential invariant under the moves, i and ω are stationary points, because the
  gradient must be fixed by the stabiliser's rotation. Whether ω is the minimum is a question of dynamics, not of the
  threads. So τ = ω is the largest finite symmetry chosen, which is what your tag "given τ = ω" says.
- **End data.** None is forced here either. W36 showed the flat counts are end contributions that a gap removes, and the
  physical end law (GENESIS FK10) is open by ruling 3 and belongs to the audit lane.

**5. B1617, read as sealed and not replayed.** This seat will verify it with its own construction after your read-out,
as with W35, W37 and W38. One remark from the normal form, conditional and not a prediction of your cells:
- if the residual group's image in S₄ is the 3-cycle alone, T splits into three lines with distinct characters;
- if it also contains the normal Klein group, the image is A₄, which is irreducible on T, and the Dirac term allows

**1. Which tensor carries the masses.** B1615 reads a Dirac mass on T̄ ⊗ T, a left-handed field in T paired with a
right-handed field in T. Given Λ (W27; the owner's ruling 2), every left-handed state of a generation is a holomorphic
zero mode, so every left-handed Weyl field of the 27 (Q, u^c, d^c, L, e^c, ν^c) is in T, and the right-handed fields
are their conjugates, in T̄. A mass term pairs two left-handed fields (Q u^c H, L e^c H, L ν^c H, ν^c ν^c), so its flavour
tensor is T ⊗ T with the Higgs's representation.

**2. With E₆'s cubic and one 27 Higgs the Yukawas are symmetric** (the d-symbol is symmetric, and so is the Weyl
bilinear). So every sector's masses lie in Sym² T, B1616's tensor. On the record's group (lifts in SU(2)):
- a Higgs without flavour gives no mass at all, since (Sym² T)^G = 0 (B1615's "(1, 1, 1) from a singlet" was the
  T̄ ⊗ T reading);
- a Higgs from the generations' own 27s gives none, since (T ⊗ T ⊗ T)^G = 0;
- any mass needs a Higgs carrying c̄², where T ⊗ T = c² ⊗ (1 + 2 + 3 + 3′) and c(L)² = −i;
- along every residual, all irreducibles at once, each spectrum has an exactly degenerate pair or vanishes (§38,
  point 2). So given Λ, the residual-vacuum reading dies for the charged fermions too, not only for the neutrinos.
- With an antisymmetric part as well (a 351 Higgs, outside the minimal Yukawa), the whole T ⊗ T fixed spaces are, by
  hand: the circulants along RL (three free masses); (0, |p|, |q|) along L and R; nothing along RRL.

**3. The premise B1615, B1616 and W39 share: the character c.** "No invariant in Sym² T" and "nothing fixed along
RRL" rest on c² ≠ 1.
- c² is fixed when no gauge transformation commuting with 𝕎's holonomy acts on T by a phase. In W24's E₈ embedding the
  centraliser is F₄ × SU(2), with centre ℤ₂, so c² is physical there.
- In a frame with such a U(1), c is shifted by gauge, and the Higgs's own charge carries the phase. Sym² T then gains
  the singlet (three equal masses), and along RRL a 2 × 2 block of symmetric masses is free (by hand).
- Which frame holds is GENESIS FK11's datum, open under ruling 2. **Asked of main:** carry the frame on B1615 and
  B1616's statements, as "given Λ" is carried.

**4. For B1617's read-out** (to read with your cells, not a prediction of them). If Z3 is read on T̄ ⊗ T, the Λ tensor
changes it. Let a residual element act on T as c_U times a 3-cycle. Given Λ with the minimal Higgs, the fixed symmetric
matrices are then:
- one diagonal entry and one off-diagonal pair when c_U² is a cube root of unity, so masses (|a|, |b|, |b|);

(weight 0), as B1615 to B1617's Z3 take them. In B1618's reading the Yukawas are modular forms of weight k, and the
automorphy factor can supply the phase c̄². So "a Higgs without flavour gives no mass" is a weight-0 statement. The
tensor point stands at every weight: given Λ, a mass pairs two fields of T.

**3. The zero modes, in full (you asked twice; they were in §32, point 4).**
- **The form.** At the common point (a ↦ i, b ↦ j), F = (f(z), f(z + τ)) dz with f(z) = θ₃(z | 2τ) / √θ₁(z | τ).
  - Continued from the base point 0.3305 + 0.3458τ along straight paths, F(z + 1) = ρ_Q(a) F(z) and
    F(z + τ) = ρ_Q(b) F(z).
  - It is holomorphic, with square-integrable square-root poles at the puncture.
- **The three modes.** In parity p's block the mode is C_p F, with C_p = j, i, ij. The code is
  `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_holomorphic_triplet_periods.py`.
- **The weight (a READING, by hand; W41 will check it numerically).**
  - The numerator is θ₃(z | 2τ) = θ_{1,0}(τ, z/2), with partner θ₂(z | 2τ) = θ_{1,1}(τ, z/2). That is the index-1
    Jacobi theta pair at z/2: weight ½, index ¼, a vector-valued form under the Weil representation.
  - The denominator √θ₁(z | τ) has weight ¼ and index ¼.
  - So f has weight ¼ and index 0, as a section of a flat bundle must. The one-form F = f dz has weight −¾. Its
    Hodge–Riemann norm scales as |cτ + d|^{−3/2}, so the normalised field's Kähler weight is ¾.
  - The multipliers are roots of unity from θ₁'s eighth roots, square-rooted.
- **Why the weave's c appears.** The modes' cohomology classes stay on fixed parity lines at every τ (W21's second
  route: the same subspace at three τ, one line per block). So a move acts on the modes by its automorphy factor times
  c ⊗ S, the weave's group in W38's normal form, and the scalar period carries the weight.
  - At ω, U's automorphy factor is (1 + ω)^{−3/4} = e^{−iπ/4} (a turn of −⅛) times its multiplier. So the modes'
    U-phases at ω are W40's eigen-turns shifted by that.

determining the weight, the multipliers and the modes' U-phases at ω. That is the input your S95 asks for.

## 47. Added 2026-10-09: the triplet's index on the weave's own surface (W45); to the audit lane

To main, and to the audit lane. The dossier's W45 carries the full record. Everything here is given Λ (the owner's
ruling 2).

**1. The index on the weave's own surface is 0 for both hands (W45; the rule 6a8ac3640 first; one run).**
- **The object and the operator.** The object is W19's M₁,₂. The operator is the four-dimensional Dirac operator
  twisted by 𝕎, with the only spin structure every move keeps, the fibre's odd one. Kodaira's formula forces weight
  3/2. By Leray the index is χ_k(ρ) = dim M_k(ρ) − dim S_{2−k}(ρ^∨), with ρ = ρ_T for one hand and ρ̄_T for the other.
- **T lives on the metaplectic cover.** (L R⁻¹ L)⁴ is conjugation by a b⁻¹ a⁻¹ b. On T it acts as −I for every lift
  sign, while the inner automorphisms' lifts send that word to I. So T is not a local system on M₁,₂ itself, and only
  half-integral weights carry it.
- **The formula** (Borcherds, Skoruppa) was calibrated exactly: 1, 0 and 2 for the trivial representation, 1 for η, and
  1 for W41's Weil representation. Of the four identifications of S and T with S̃^{±1} and L^{±1}, exactly the two the
  braid relation allows are integral: ρ_T = (S̃, L⁻¹) and its dual (S̃⁻¹, L).
- **The index at the weight the geometry forces is 0.** χ_{3/2}(ρ_T) = 0. ρ̄_T is not allowed at 3/2, and at its
  nearest weights χ_½ = χ_{5/2} = 0.
- **A second route** used q-expansions, with the S relation imposed on the arc (gaps of nine orders). It finds every one
  of these spaces zero, so there are no zero modes at all, chiral or vector-like.
- **Three first appears at weight 23/2** (ρ_T) and 25/2 (ρ̄_T). The rule's "15/2 or more" held but was loose:
  χ_{15/2}(ρ_T) = 2.

  (threads) and W20 (an Euler characteristic, not chiral), and the weave's own route to GENESIS FK11's index is closed in
  that reading. GENESIS FK11 stays open.

  at the weave's one cusp remains. Allowing a pole of order m_j on the component with exponent λ_j changes the index
  by Σ m_j.

  3 = dim T. So n units give 3n.

  form the flat bundle ρ_T, whose Riemann–Roch number at weight 3/2 is 0.

To main. The dossier's W49 carries the full record. The owner turned to the free numbers: look for a state that breaks
the weave's symmetry and is forced by the principle, and name the obstruction and attack it.

**1. The result (the rule cd174afa7 first; one run; every cell as predicted).**
- **T is one zero mode three times.** T meets each parity block of V in a line, and the quaternion intertwiners send
  the three lines to one line ℓ of the spin doublet, with Q = +2 on it. So T = ℓ ⊗ M, where M is the three imaginary
  units i, j, k.
- **Geometry acts on ℓ, flavour on M.** Every lift acts on T as a scalar on ℓ times a rotation of the units, its
  conjugation of i, j and k. The lifts of L and R give your B1620 group, W42's 96, exactly. The Hodge–Riemann form is
  2·I on M.
- **Three or split.** The puncture conditions that keep ±3 are exactly the products, 0 and L₆. V2 and V4 are entangled
  (every vector of V2 has singular values in the ratio √2 : 1) and give ∓1.
- **Disclosed:** the rule's M4 inference ("generic rank 2, so not a product") holds for V2 but not for V4. A support
  test decided both; the prediction was unchanged.

**2. What it shows (a reading on those facts, given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **Schur's lemma, as geometry.** Data built from the local system and the surface alone are unchanged by GL(M), so they
  give three degenerate, orthogonal generations. An end condition that could tell the generations apart does not keep
  three. That is the geometric form of your §3.

**3. A premise to check, found while writing this up (post hoc; a reading; W50 will test it, rule first).**
- **T-TAU-ONLY-PERMUTATION uses the inner automorphisms by a and by b as symmetries that fix every τ.** So do B1617's
  order-48 residual and this seat's W40 and §46, which verified them.
- **The inner automorphisms by a and b are not words in L and R.**
  - ⟨L, R⟩ is a quotient of B₃ that maps onto SL(2, ℤ), and the kernel of B₃ → SL(2, ℤ) is generated by Δ⁴.
  - Δ⁴ is conjugation by a b⁻¹ a⁻¹ b (W45), which acts on T as −I.
  - They enter with the sign (σ L σ L⁻¹ = conj(a⁻¹)) or with the swap (P L P R⁻¹ = conj(b)), by hand.
- **On the owner's ruled branch** (even ticks and positivity, the weave ⟨L, R⟩), at a generic τ the residual on T is
  only the scalar Z. At i it is generated by Δ (T splits as a line plus a plane), and at ω by σ₁σ₂ (three lines).
- **Your computations stand.** The question is which group they are about. If the weave is ⟨L, R⟩, couplings in τ
  alone are constrained at a generic τ only by scalars, and the modular route to the flavour numbers is open again.
  W50 will compute whether such couplings, as modular forms on M, give mixing that is not a permutation.
- **The ask.** Do you read the inner automorphisms as moves of the ruled weave (for instance through the weave's surface
  M₁,₂, where they move the records' base point), or only on the sign and swap branches?

  there (Osgood, Phillips and Sarnak). i is a saddle of each.

  to the cusp.

  canonical supplies.

  powers of q^{1/8}, and the count depends on the cusp's end data (W45 to W48).

lines (≈ 1.551i and ≈ ½ + 2.224i), where the masses would be non-degenerate. A principle that extremizes a coupling's
strength would select them. None is on record.

## 61. Added 2026-10-13: does the gauge side on record tell the sectors apart (W55)? No

To main. The dossier's W55 carries the full record; the rule (397e92bfc) came first; every cell went as predicted.

**1. The tie.** With one flavour matrix λ serving every coupling, F-MC's E₆ cubic (your THE_CLAIM §1: matter mass in
27³ ⊃ 16·16·10, the doublet in the 10 ⊂ 27, with the rank-closing ⟨1⟩ and ⟨ν^c⟩) gives the down-type matrix
P ⊗ λ on (d, D) × (d^c, D^c), P = [[v_d, 0], [n, s]], and the charged leptons Pᵀ ⊗ λ. Their singular values are
σ_a(P)·σ_i(λ), so the light down quarks and charged leptons carry the same ratios. SO(10) with one 10_H and minimal
SU(5) tie them the same way.

running allowance would admit it. This is the classic down–lepton mismatch, met in the record's own frame.

Standard Model frame, separate doublets whose weights are free (the tie k_d = k_e keeps (5, 5, 5), (5, 7, 7) and
(7, 7, 7), with 23 to 29 parameters). Each tells the sectors apart only with new free numbers.

**4. The reading.** What tells the sectors apart is the Higgs sector's flavour structure: where the Higgs sits relative
to T, with what coupling and weight. That is a dictionary datum (GENESIS FK11), supplied by neither the weave nor
F-MC. For THE_CLAIM §1's "matter mass in the cubic": with one flavour matrix it is excluded by the down–lepton
mismatch, and it survives only with Higgs fields carrying flavour or their own couplings (W39 named the 351′). Ask:
does main read F-MC's cubic with one flavour matrix, and if not, which Higgs content does F-MC intend?

To main. The dossier's "Reading: couplings as overlaps on the shared fibre" (C0 of the seat's plan) carries it.
Nothing is computed by a script. Points 2 and 3 are proved by hand.

   no generation dependence, and the flavour content sits in the Higgs's tensor (W56).
2. **The neutral channel vanishes.** The common point's invariant pairing is the antisymmetric ε, and ε(ℓ, ℓ) = 0
   pointwise. In the T ⊗ T reading (W39), neither a neutral Wilson-line nor a modulus fluctuation couples the three
   alike. The Higgs must sit in Sym² ρ_Q = ⊕_p χ_p: it must carry a parity. In B1615's T̄ ⊗ T reading the neutral
   channel survives and gives three degenerate generations.
3. **No normalizable massless mode carries a parity.** H¹(F₂; χ_p) is one class, and it restricts nontrivially to the
   puncture, so the parabolic and L² parts are 0. Such a Higgs lives at the puncture's end (GENESIS GAP2's kind, as
   GENESIS FK10 at the cusp).

   - (a) the boundary class as a tagged end condition; the couplings then become numbers c_p(τ) times the Higgs's
     direction;
   - (b) a Higgs from outside the fibre, which gives back the free coefficients;
   - (c) the one-loop potential (D1) first.

   for the ±3 and the flavour structure does not follow from end data alone.

   the cusp's six lines (c < 0) or on the eight (1, ω, ω²)-type lines (c > 0). On every special line the Yukawa has
   rank one: one heavy generation and two massless.

   from the cone of size |a·a|/|a|² ≈ m₂/m₃, and nothing computed supplies it.

     m_E = 9/14.

exact.

   - in SU(6), the commutant of colour × weak;

   - such that the Standard Model's five fields appear with their charges.

     |Y| = ½.

     (b, a, ab).

   gauge embedding they are not three alike generations, and the generations must live in the matter's sections.

   duties remain the lane's: a global completion that keeps U(1)_em (the published one leaves G₂), and the global

     sector) from a Higgs in M to every Higgs at the puncture.

     W64 (§77) labelled both frames, and W65 to W69 followed. The surface frame's overlap integral is not on the seat's
     record. It is the step main names.
2. **S121's ask: does anything on the seat's record carry an up–down distinction into the fibre? No.**
   - **Under Λ,** a generation's whole 27 sits in one parity sector. Its up and down quarks carry one spin structure
     and share R. A sector with another spin-structure assignment (P-129's second premise) would leave Λ, not refine
     it.
   - **W59's 3-torsion line is the colour centre's.** Q, u^c and d^c see it alike, so it can split quarks from leptons,
     not up from down. W60's full potential then put the line at a half period, where the matter sees nothing.

   - **The moves: allowed, not forced.** The moves fix a flat U(1) line on the shared fibre only when it is trivial
     (§66). A non-trivial line is a state of the field, like τ.
   - **The background: compatible.** Under W70's H-charge, hypercharge commutes with the quaternion holonomies, so
     the line can be switched on without changing the puncture's holonomy.
   - **Λ: respected in Λ's frame.** There hypercharge is an element of E₆ acting on each 27 alike, so it does not mix
     the parity sectors.
   - **But the frames differ.** In the audit lane's E₈ embedding, the hypercharge the holonomies keep separates the
     three quaternion units (W70's N3). The weave's moves permute the units, so they permute its six choices, and
     there the units cannot be the generations. Main's lead lives in Λ's frame.
4. **The charge normalisation.**
   - **The Wilson charges** are 6Y ∈ ℤ: 1, −4, 2, −3, 6 and 0 for Q, u^c, d^c, L, e^c and ν^c, and +3 and −3 for H_u
     and H_d. Then exp(2πi w·6Y) has period 1 in w.

     735fb0513 before the read-out): k_Y = |Y|²/|T₃|² is 14/3 for every hypercharge the quaternion holonomies keep.

     splits a block, so the holonomies break it.

     embedding's scale. With the Standard Model's one-loop running from M_Z, α_Y⁻¹/α₂⁻¹ never reaches 14/3 above M_Z;
     it reaches 5/3 at 10¹³ GeV.

   drift is 0. The census scores every flat end datum in a stated family:

content.

   internal factor to be even under the puncture. That is B1649's S, and its zero holds there. In the audit lane's
   frame the puncture is the weak centre, so a Standard Model Higgs is odd, but the even stalk's matter is on neither
   record. P-129's first premise is therefore decided by the frame, and the frame question is P-108.
2. **No residue at the cusp (exact).** In ρ_R a dot product pairs only e_i with e_i and v₊ with v₋, both at exponents
   ≡ ¼. So B1644's coupling between two matter modes carries η¹²'s multiplier: its q-exponents lie in ½ + ℤ, and a
   holomorphic, cusp-localised Yukawa vanishes identically.

   - Written as a·G_αβ, the coupling defines G_αβ of weight 5/2 in ρ_R ⊗ χ_i, where χ_i is the character with T ↦ i.
   - The Higgs's surface mode of that type is unique: χ_{5/2} = 1, cuspidal, with integer coefficients, q^{⅛}(1 + q −
     8q² + …) on v₊.
   - The Petersson pairing Y_αβ = ⟨G_αβ, h⟩ over the three chiral modes F_i, F₊, F₋ (W64, W68) agrees between two
     settings to 5 × 10⁻¹¹. **It has rank 3.**

     fibre frame at one τ.
4. **But no mass ratio is defined yet.** Each chiral mode carries its unit at the cusp as a growing term (coefficient 1
   at −⅞, −⅛, −⅝), so its Petersson norm diverges. Y's singular values depend on how the modes are normalised, and
   none is derived. The seat makes no comparison with data. The masses wait on a source at the cusp that makes the
   modes normalisable: P024's edge and GENESIS FK10's unit again.

   at τ = i.

## 88. Added 2026-10-14: W74 — the surface frame is CP-invariant; I-18's Yukawa half

To main. W72's Yukawa came out real, so W74 asked why, and what it means for the strong phase (I-18, B1243; priced by
B1246 at the dictionary and the Yukawa phase). The rule (cf80a886f) first, then one run. All three cells went as
predicted.

1. **The involution.** For a section f, set f*(τ) = C · conj f(−τ̄) with C = diag(1, 1, −1).
   - Exactly: C L C = L⁻¹, C P C = P, and C conj B[l] = B[l] on all three eigenlines.
   - The S-phase condition holds at the weights the surface frame uses (−½, 3/2 and 7/2 in ρ_R, and 5/2 in ρ′), and
     fails at ½ and 1 as controls.
   - So every section is fixed by the map, its coefficients are real, and the Petersson Yukawa is real.
   - W72's stored Y is real to 1.5 × 10⁻¹³, and h's coefficients to 3 × 10⁻³⁴.
2. **For I-18.** With the record's one coupling, arg det(M_u M_d) = 2 arg det Y = 0, so θ̄ = θ. In the surface frame,
   I-18's Yukawa half is paid, and it rests on the dictionary alone, as it read before B1246. It is not earned.
3. **The price.** The same symmetry gives J = 0, and the data need J ≈ 3 × 10⁻⁵. Whatever splits the sectors must also
   break CP.

     reads "D1 given an unselected vacuum", and the debt is booked on D6 in the same column, as main does.
   - The breaking must come from the vacuum alone. A completion of the moves by a chosen background gauge (F3's
     non-scalar completion, F4's U(1)_⊥ phase) is a choice of the theory, not of the vacuum. So it is a D6 entry
     ("supplied") even when D1 then holds exactly.
   - **A consequence for F1.** The only vacua any dynamics on record selects are τ = ω and the cusp (W59, W62, W67),
     and neither has a hierarchy (W65, W67). So in F1, D1 with a dynamically selected vacuum conflicts with D5 on the
     record, not only with D6. The D1 cell should say so.
2. **Lemma A survives, but its proof needs both halves** (W82, by explicit matrices).
   - A parallel charge commutes with the holonomy Q8, whose inner automorphisms act on the units as V₄. That makes it
     diagonal (commutant 3).
   - The moves permute the units transitively (S₃). That makes the diagonal constant (with V₄: commutant 1).
   - Transitivity alone allows a + bJ (commutant 2), a democratic split 1 ⊕ 2, which is unalike. A non-transitive
     control gives 2.
   - So lemma A holds wherever the holonomy is the record's Q8 and the moves act on the units through S₃. The proof as
     drafted should add the V₄ half. T-A's control should include S₃ without V₄.
3. **F4: the algebra survives, the frame does not, on three counts from the record.**
   - **The algebra, by hand.** The completion phase is the same on every generator. So every homogeneous relation of
     B₄/Z holds, and (XYZ)⁴, of length 12, gets e^{iπ} on 2_Q and e^{−2πi} on the singlet, which cancels −I₂. The
     commutant in su(3)_⊥ is 1 and 3_⊥ has no invariants, given an irreducible image on 2_Q. The parallel algebra
     e₆ ⊕ u(1) stands.
   - **(a) The renormalizable vertex is zero, not merely antisymmetric.** B1642's R1 (Fᵀ ε F = 0, exactly) is this
     seat's C0 and W61: all three zero modes share one line ℓ, and ε(ℓ, ℓ) = 0. So the S = 1 pairing vanishes before
     E₆'s symmetric cubic is used, and the sign question is moot for S = 1.
     - The flavon operator is B1644's Y(a) = aRᵀ + Raᵀ − (a·R)𝟙. It is symmetric, a product of two antisymmetric
       insertions, so it survives a symmetric cubic.
     - What the record does not settle is whether F4's gauge index 2_Q and the fibre's spin doublet are one index, as
       at the record's common point, or two. If two, each carries its own ε, and their number decides the vertex's
       symmetry. **So B1642's internal factor is not shown to be the whole internal sign.** T-C1 should state the
       count, and its convention check on a known case should include it.
   - **(b) Its vacua break D1.** 3_⊥ has no invariants (main's own prediction). So the Higgs in (27, 1) carries the
     completion's phase e^{−iπ/6} per generator, and the flavon (1, 3_Q) = M has no direction that O fixes. Both vacuum
     values break the moves. F4's D1 therefore holds for the gauge algebra, and for the vacuum only as spontaneous
     breaking, with F1's D6 debt: nothing selects ⟨H⟩ or ⟨Φ_M⟩.
   - **(c) The down–lepton tie: F4 fails D4 on the data.** In F4 every Yukawa runs through E₆'s cubic with a 27 Higgs;
     the flavon is an E₆ singlet.
     - W56 (exact, on random tensors with one to three Higgs 27s, all vacuum values): in E₆'s cubic no Higgs content
       in 27s tells the down quarks from the charged leptons, because their spectra are equal. The literature states
       the same (Babu, Bajc and Susič, arXiv:1504.00904).
     - W55: the data exclude the tie, χ² ≥ 106 at the 10% running allowance; the tie would need 48%.
     - So F4 as drawn fails D4 against the data, unless its Yukawa sector carries E₆ breaking (a 78 or 351′
       insertion, as W39's 351′). Each brings new free numbers (D6).
     - **Asked:** a D4 cell for F4 citing W55 and W56, and a seventh clause for the parent: *split the down quarks
       from the charged leptons; no E₆ cubic with 27 Higgs content alone can.*
4. **Cells this seat would grade differently.**
   - **F1, D1:** add the D1–D5 conflict (item 1).
   - **F1, D8:** W75's "no free size" is scoped (§100, W81). It holds for the ordinary uniformizing Higgs field. The
     lane's condensate is spinor-valued, a different type, and no map is earned.
   - **F2, D2:** "given Λ and the unit" understates it. W47 shows that nothing on the weave's surface supplies the
     unit: no flat line bundle and no natural cusp condition. W48 shows that a c = 24 source from outside would. Grade:
     CONDITIONAL on a non-flat source from outside the weave.
   - **F3, D4:** add W81 and W82. In the lane's frame the condensate's root lies in SU(2)_Q doublets. Under every
     admissible Y, the neutral |Y| = ½ condensates are one SU(2)_Q doublet and its conjugate. And per the lane's audit
     the condensate is not an ordinary Higgs one-form, so "the coupling to matter is not constructed" stands, for a
     sharper reason.
5. **Evidence main missed.**
   - W55 and W56, for F4 (item 3c).
   - C0 and W61 with B1642's R1, for F4's vertex (item 3a).
   - W79, for main's selection-principle item 2 to chat1, which cites "near the cusp (B1637's fits, Im τ ≈ 2.6–7)". By
     W79 the fits above Im τ ≈ 5 are most likely artifacts of main's evaluator, and W80 is deciding it.

6. **The verdict: this seat would bank outcome 2, with the parent as a labelled specification.**

7. **The hook (main's ask from §92), answered.**

     with quartic 2, and a background r = (v + u)H_Q induces 4vut² with the sign of v. It is not a stationary E₈
     solution.
   - **Two condensates in the 112 (W82):** not as one real E₈ field. Under every admissible Y the neutral |Y| = ½
     condensates are one weak doublet with Y = −½ carrying a weave-doublet index, plus its conjugate. So one
     condensate gives H_u = εH_d* in the weave's index, and v_a = d†σ_a d is real: m₂/m₃ ≥ ½ (B1645).
     - Two independent condensates need two complex fields, such as the lane's q and r, one per hypercharge sign.
     - Their misalignment det[u, d]² is then free, and nothing on record fixes it. It is one more "which" for
       chat1's selection principle (main's item 3 to chat1).

## 103. Added 2026-10-14: one of the tribunal's overturning items answered by a dichotomy (W83): in the lane's frame the moves and a hypercharge meet only if its family index is not the weave's three

To main and the audit lane. Main's draft lists, among what could overturn it, "a gauge completion of the moves that
keeps SU(2)_w and Y, or a proof that none exists in the lane's E₈ frame". W83 (script first; exact) answers it with a
dichotomy.
1. **Every hypercharge lies in the lane's su(6).** The roots of e₈ orthogonal to colour and the weak root number 30,
   an A₅ containing the lane's chain, so the commutant of colour and SU(2)_w is that su(6). A completion that keeps
   both acts there, up to centres.
2. **On its 6 = 3 ⊗ 2_q, a trace-free Y commuting with the holonomy 1₃ ⊗ Q8 and the moves exists only if the moves act
   on the family index other than through O.** The dimension is 0 through O, 1 through S₃ only (the democratic split),
   and 8 if trivial (the whole su(3) of that index). The seat's prediction for the trivial case was 2, which was wrong:
   it counted only the Cartan. Recorded as failed as worded.
3. **So the item resolves by the dictionary.**
   - If the lane's family index is the weave's three (the units M, on which the moves act through O, W49), no
     completion keeping colour and SU(2)_w keeps a hypercharge: the "none exists" main asked for, given that
     identification.
   - If a completion keeps both, the lane's family index is not the weave's three.
   - Either way, the parent's clause 1 gains a precise form: its family index is the weave's three, and its
     hypercharge is family-blind (lemma A) and lies outside any su(6) whose 6 is 3 ⊗ 2_q.
4. **To the audit lane.** Is the lane's family index meant to be the weave's three, with the moves acting through O as
   in your completion? If not, which three is it?

     twisted fields there are standard. The puncture is still distinguished: it is the fibre's hole and is fixed by
     every move, while the moves permute the three half-periods (B1651's two covariant loci).

     selection relates to B1651's localised couplings is open; the seat does not claim an equivalence.

## 106. Added 2026-10-14: chat1's thread frame checked (W84): its numbers reproduce at 60 digits, and its Lagrange law tends deep in the cusp to the units' q-powers, not to a constant slope 7π/3

To chat1, with main and the audit lane copied. chat1's thread frame and Lagrange law are built on this seat's W72 forms,
so the seat checked them as W84, rule first: one run, every cell as predicted.
1. **Your numbers reproduce.** On this seat's own trapezoid integrator, at 60 digits, with nodes at half steps from
   yours, LR, LLRR, LLR, LLRLR and LLLR agree with your banked ln(m₂/m₃) and ln(m₁/m₃) to 5 × 10⁻¹¹.
2. **The slope is not constant.**
   - Along L^n R (n = 1 to 8, y_max up to 4.90), the local slopes of −ln(m₂/m₃) are 7.64, 7.33, 6.68, 6.45, 6.41, 6.42
     and 6.43. Those of −ln(m₁/m₃) are 5.47, 8.39, 8.95, 9.05, 9.01, 9.10 and 9.34.
   - So your 7.332, fitted over y_max ≤ 3, is an average over that range. Its closeness to 7π/3 is a coincidence of the
     range.
3. **The deep-cusp law is fixed by the units' exponents.** Given H-thread.
   - K diagonalises: its normalised off-diagonal is 1.5 × 10⁻⁹ at n = 8. K_aa ∝ |q|^{−2e_a}, with
     e = (7/8, 1/8, 5/8) for (F_i, F₊, F₋).
   - So Ŷ is a Froggatt–Nielsen texture with those exponents as charges. F₊, the shallowest unit, is the heaviest state
     (your TF3 observation, at every n here).
   - It gives m₂/m₃ ∝ |q|¹ and m₁/m₃ ∝ |q|^{3/2}, with |q| = e^{−2π y_max} = e^{−πλ}, λ the Lagrange value. The last
     slopes are 2.4% above 2π and 0.9% below 3π.
   - One residual is open: the m₂/m₃ slope plateaus at 6.41–6.43 and has not visibly turned toward 2π by y = 4.6. So the
     scale and the mechanism are fixed, but the exact limit is not resolved here.
4. **What it adds to your fence.**
   - Given H-thread, a vacuum's hierarchy has no continuous free number. It is structure (the units' exponents) and one
     discrete datum (the thread's Lagrange value).
   - Sector differences would need sector-dependent units, which is your own "other weights or representations". A
     sector whose matter sits at other exponents would get other powers of the same |q|.
5. **To main.** For the tribunal's D5 and D6 in the surface frame, H-thread replaces H-end's post hoc normalisation with
   finite cycle norms. The ratios become functions of the thread, with exponents the record already has. It stays a
   hypothesis, and the frame tribunal decides whether it counts.

## 107. Added 2026-10-14: B1651's owed parabolic statement is on record (C0, item 3); on the punctured fibre a bulk Higgs carrying parity couples only through the puncture's residue, at the price of one end datum

To main, with the audit lane and chat1 copied. Fetched again after §106: main is at 6d6f7f660, the lane at f0760049f
and chat1 at 39bfc86fe, with no pushes since §105. W84 (§106) is pushed. This section is a reading, and nothing in it
is computed. It is in the weave dossier's NOTE, under "Reading: on the punctured fibre a bulk Higgs carrying parity
couples only through the puncture's residue".
1. **B1651's owed item is on record.** B1651 owes "the punctured, parabolic version" of its bulk statement, and this
   seat's C0 reading (2026-10-13, item 3) proves it.
   - For each parity character χ, H¹(F₂; χ) = ℂ, and a cocycle's value on the puncture loop maps it isomorphically onto
     the boundary.
   - So the parabolic part is 0, and so is the L² cohomology. No parabolic or L² bulk Higgs couples.
   - B1651's step 2 can cite it.
2. **The Dolbeault form turns B1642's hypotheses into one end datum.**
   - The zero modes' pairing in each channel, α = v dz with a simple pole, is fixed by its residue. Res_p is an
     isomorphism, since K ⊗ χ has no cohomology.
   - A (0, 1) Higgs carrying parity (C0, item 2) satisfies ∫ ∂̄φ ∧ α = −2πi φ(p) Res_p α, by Stokes.
     - So the overlap is a function of the Higgs's class only if the gauge functions vanish at the puncture: a framing
       there.
     - On the closed fibre the term is absent.
   - Framed, the Higgs's classes are H¹(O(−p) ⊗ χ) ≅ χ_p, one value at the puncture.
     - They have a smooth representative supported as near p as one likes.
     - It couples as −2πi h Res_p α, which is B1642's and B1644's residue coupling.
   - So B1642's two disclosed hypotheses become one end datum, the framing, for a bulk Higgs carrying parity: the
     localization at the puncture and the coupling through the residue.
   - Read flat instead, C0's class is v̄ dz̄.
     - Its profile grows like 1/|z|, and its overlap diverges logarithmically, with a coefficient set by |Res_p α|².
     - That needs a cutoff, which is again an end datum.
3. **For B1651's step 4, a suggested refinement of its reading.**
   - "The puncture is not forced" holds for point Higgs fields (P1).
   - For a bulk Higgs carrying parity the puncture is forced as the locus, at the price of one end datum. W47 found
     that nothing on the weave's surface supplies that kind of datum (GENESIS FK10 at the cusp, GAP2 at the fibre's
     puncture).
   - So the choice is between two kinds of field, not between points.
   - In the tribunal's terms, F1's premise "the Higgs at the puncture" then follows from the field's kind. Its framing
     is a D6 entry ("supplied"), in the same column as W47's unit.
4. **After B1651 runs,** this seat will check its cells U1–U5 on its own code, as the owner's rule asks, unless main
   prefers otherwise. The tribunal's asks in §101 stand.

To main, with chat1 and the audit lane copied. Main asked in S129 and S130: "If you check one thing, check §1.2 of

1. **B1654's section 1 holds on this seat's code, and it has a closed form.**
   - **c_r(τ) = −2π ln((π/2)|θ_r(0 | τ)|²).** It is proved by Parseval in the strip, with Euler's and Gauss's product
     identities. Three routes agree: the series to 10⁻⁵⁹, and a polar quadrature of the definition to 4 × 10⁻¹⁴.
   - **D(τ) = −θ₃⁴h(λ)/(4πη⁶),** with λ = θ₂⁴/θ₃⁴ and h(λ) = λ ln|λ| + (1 − λ) ln|1 − λ|. So
     |D|/|R|² = π|h(λ)|/(1 + |λ| + |1 − λ|).
   - **What follows:**
     - main's 1.09 at i is (π/2) ln 2;
     - N5(c)'s "no zero within about 0.02 of a node" becomes exact: D vanishes on ω's orbit and nowhere else in ℍ;

     - near ω, |D|/|R|² = (π²√3/6)|θ₃(ω)|⁴·|τ − ω| = 2.9492·|τ − ω| in every direction (post hoc, by hand after the
       read-out);

     P4″ gives twice P4's relative lift.

## 149. Added 2026-10-16: to main: the hostile read of P9 draft 3 (S140, the owner's request), with the checks on this seat's code

To main; the audit lane and the web seat for information.

**How it was read.** §3, §4, §5, §7's one-Higgs census, every reading of structure as physics, and the price of I-31 to
I-34. The tex sources and the built PDF are main's at 4cc3658a8, with page numbers from the PDF.
- Every number this seat could recompute was recomputed on its own code:
  `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_paper_read_checks.py`, run once, output committed as written.
- The checks were first run in scratch while reading (the NOTE's W99).
- **No BLOCKER was found in these sections.** The findings below are ranked MAJOR, MAJOR (understated), then MINOR.

### MAJOR

1. **§5, the norm of the boundary class, p. 15.** "at the cusp D/(q|ρ|²) = 8π² Im τ + 8π(1 − 4 ln 2)"
   - **Why.** True only on Re τ = 0. Since D(τ + 1) = iD(τ) and q(τ + 1) = −q, the left side gains a factor −i per
     unit shift, and the right side does not. At τ = ½ + 4i the left side is 174.05 − 174.05i, against 271.28.
   - **Fix.** "On Re τ = 0, D/(q|ρ|²) = 8π² Im τ + 8π(1 − 4 ln 2) + O(Im τ · q). In general, with τ = x + iy,
     D/(q|ρ|²) = 8π e^{−iπx/2}(πy − 4 ln 2 + (1 + e^{−2πix})/2) + O(y|q|)." This is checked to 7 × 10⁻³ at y = 4.
   - **Owned.** The slip is this seat's. W87's rule states the expansion for τ = iy, but the NOTE's summary of W87
     dropped the condition. B1654's findings (its line on the cusp, "|q|(8π² · Im τ + 8π(1 − 4 ln 2))") state it for
     every τ. The NOTE now carries a dated correction; B1654's line wants the same.
2. **Table 1, p. 34, and §6's inputs, p. 19.** The triplet postulate's discharge: "an even-dimensional object with a
   non-flat bundle whose index is the count".
   - **Why.** That is the count's discharge (I-26, I-34). I-31's own discharge, in the identification ledger, is the
     dictionary: which fields carry T (GENESIS FK11). The ledger prices I-31 "as its own source", because earning the
     count "would leave this unpaid".
   - **Fix.** "A derivation of which fields carry T (the dictionary), or an obstruction; an index for the count does not
     pay it."
3. **Table 1, p. 34, against p. 7's "Table 1 lists every input this paper uses".**
   - **Why.** Three inputs of §5 and N5 are missing:
     - B1654's end condition at the puncture, used by the electron's zero, the symmetric point's law in L, and N5;
     - τ = ω with the eighteen lines, used by the electron's zero and N5;
     - equal framed data, used by the twisted half bound.
     The dictionary row also drops I-32's second clause, the Higgs line as the electroweak Higgs.
   - **Fix.** Add three rows with status "chosen":
     - the end condition, discharged by an end mechanism, noting that B1667 fixes the radius at order one;
     - τ = ω and the lines, a selection (§8);
     - equal framed data.
     Add the Higgs clause to the dictionary row.
   - **The price paragraph is right.** "Three of the nine" holds, because I-32 → I-23 → I-13 and I-33 → I-13 are one
     source.
4. **§7, opening, p. 20.** "The structure of the previous sections is forced."
   - **Why.** §3's part 3, §4's hand, §5 and §6 hold on stated inputs: the triplet postulate, M₁,₂ as a reading, a
     frame and a chosen domain. The paper's own scope notes say so, and its conventions bar "forced" without scope.
   - **Fix.** "The structure of the previous sections holds on the inputs of Table 1, and it fixes no number."
5. **§4, the remark on the modulus, p. 12.** "the flavour numbers are the values of vector-valued modular forms for ρ
   at τ"
   - **Why.** This does not follow from the stabiliser statement before it, and the record's frames contradict it:
     - the fibre frame's couplings need the end-regularised norms (D is real-analytic, the paper's own remark, p. 17);
     - the twisted frame's Higgs is free;
     - the surface frame's kinetic matrix is a Petersson integral (W72, W95).
   - **Fix.** "…and the couplings at τ are built from vector-valued forms for ρ at τ, together with norms that are not
     holomorphic."
6. **§4, the question, p. 10.** "with no modular input, reproduce the symmetry that the modular flavour literature
   chooses"
   - **Why.** ρ = χ_η²¹ ⊗ ρ_u, and the twist changes the group and the weights:
     - its image has order 96, twice the 48 of S₄′ that ρ_u generates (recomputed);
     - its allowed weights are 3/2 mod 2, so ρ has no forms of weight one.
     The weight-one S₄′ triplet of the cited models is ρ_u, the untwisted form. The scope note states the twist, but
     the headline and "Position against the literature" do not.
   - **Fix.** "Reproduce the level-four theta multiplier that modular flavour models use, up to an η twist that doubles
     the image to order 96 and moves the weights to 3/2 mod 2."

### MAJOR (understated): two proofs offered

7. **§3, Theorem T2 part 2 and its scope, p. 9.** "an all-length statement is not claimed"
   - **It holds for every word.**
     - The Fricke maps fix the common point, so ρ∘φ has ρ's character, and ρ∘φ = gρg⁻¹ with g in the normaliser of Q₈
       in SU(2), which is 2O.
     - The class of g in Out(Q₈) ≅ S₃ is φ mod 2. So g ∈ 2T∖Q₈ exactly when tr φ is odd, g ∈ Q₈ when φ ≡ 1 mod 2,
       and g ∈ 2O∖2T for a transposition. When the trace is odd, ⟨i, j, g⟩ = 2T.
   - **Checked** on all 8,190 positive words to length 12, with the conjugator's class in 2O as predicted:
     - 3,636 words of odd trace, all in 2T∖Q₈;
     - 1,824 trivial mod 2, all in Q₈;
     - 2,730 transpositions, all in 2O∖2T.
   - **Fix.** State part 2 for every word, a weave statement with no bound. The census law on the prime above 3 stays
     a census.
8. **§5, the period phase and its census law, pp. 17–18.** "ε(γ) = ±1 the sign of the square root's continuation"
   - **ε = +1 on every thread.** Let r(s) be the ratio of (dz/ds)^{−1/2} at s + ℓ to its value at s, continued along
     the axis.
     - Then r² = (cz + d)². Since cz + d has no zero in ℍ, r = ±(cz + d) with one sign for every s.
     - As s → −∞, both points tend to the repelling fixed point x. There the two tangent directions agree, and cx + d
       is an eigenvalue of γ, positive because a word in L and R has positive trace.
     - So r = +(cz + d), and ψ(s + ℓ) = i^(#L−#R) ψ(s) on every thread.
   - **Checked** by continuing the tangent on all 745 threads to length 12: +1 on every one.
   - **Fix.** Drop ε. The theorem becomes exact for every thread, and B1666's census its numerical check.

### MINOR

9. **§3, p. 9.** "every odd-trace thread of word length at most six (54 words)". 54 counts words, rotations and powers
   included. There are 21 threads to length six, 10 of them of odd trace. Fix: "every odd-trace word of length at most
   six (54 words; the 10 odd-trace threads)". This is moot if 7 is taken.
10. **§3, the census law, p. 8.** "(cyclic rotation, inversion and the mirror)". The count 37 (16 odd, 21 even) is up
    to rotation and the exchange of L and R, as B1601 says. With inversion as well it is 34 (14 odd, 20 even). Fix: use
    B1601's words.
11. **§3, the parity lemma, p. 8.** "Any two of L,R,P generate S_3". P is not defined there; it is the swap. Later, P
    is diag(1, −1, −1) in T7 and the order-three signed permutation at the symmetric point. Fix: one letter for each.
12. **Notation, §§4–5.** Several letters carry more than one meaning:
    - ρ is both the Mp₂ representation (T3) and the theta-triplet vector (§5);
    - u is the theta vector, T4's angle variable, the coordinate at the puncture, and u_r;
    - t is the Mp₂ generator, T4's root, and the twisted bound's mass ratio.
    Fix: rename the §5 vector and T4's variables.
13. **§4, p. 10.** "Write s=H_1(RL^{-1}R) and t=H_1(L)". s and t must be classes in B₃/⟨Δ⁸⟩ = Mp₂(ℤ). Their images in
    homology lie in SL(2, ℤ), where s⁸ = 1 says nothing. Fix: "the images of RL⁻¹R and L in B₃/⟨Δ⁸⟩".
14. **§4, the proof sketch, p. 11.** "returns α=β=1". α and β are not defined. Fix: define them or drop them.
15. **§4, the scope, p. 11.** "certified numerically for d≤5". The setting paragraph on p. 10 already proves the
    identification for every d: the quotient by the irreducible quadric injects, and both sides have dimension 2d + 1.
    Fix: cite that argument and drop the hedge.
16. **§4, the remark, p. 11.** "three lines each of weight 1/3 on every parity line". What holds (recomputed) is that
    the three eigenlines of the order-12 element have squared overlap 1/3 with every parity line. Fix: say that.
17. **§5, the setting, p. 12.** "the complex 2×2 matrices with the quadratic form a·a". The Higgs space is three-
    dimensional, the span of the three units (the traceless matrices). End(ℂ²) in T4's part 4 is four-dimensional.
    Fix: "the traceless complex 2×2 matrices".
18. **§5, T4 part 1, p. 13.** "Equivalently Y(a)ρ=0=Y(a)^Tρ". The null vector gives det = 0, not conversely. Fix:
    "Indeed".
19. **§5, the residue proposition (B1650), p. 14.** "the bilinear … is a Higgs field nilpotent at every point". A scalar
    sum is called a Higgs field. Also, "the only residue" was tested only among the six assignments of units to
    half-periods. Fix: name the field whose quadratic invariant is this sum, and scope "only" to the six assignments.
20. **§5, the rank of a point coupling, p. 14.** "(r_1^2+ρ·ρ=0, …)". With ρ the theta triplet, ρ·ρ = 0 by the Jacobi
    lemma, so this would force r₁ = 0 on every line, against the quarks of the next proposition. The triplet meant
    here is the line-twisted residue. Fix: "r₁² + r·r = 0, with r the line-twisted triplet (r = ρ at w = 0)".
21. **§5, the electron's zero, p. 15.** "the eighteen flat lines fixed by the order-three move".
    - The lines are not fixed; their triples 3w are (B1656: "W59's lines have 3w fixed").
    - N5's pair "(1/9, 7/9)" on p. 23 needs the record's convention w = p₁τ − p₂ (B1656's line_w). Read as w = a + bτ
      at ω = e^{2πi/3}, 3w is not fixed.
    Fix: "the eighteen lines w = p₁τ − p₂ whose triple 3w is fixed by the order-three move".
22. **§5, the twisted half bound, p. 15.** "‖A‖=2‖M‖²". A is defined only in T7, on p. 16. Fix: a forward reference.
23. **§7, N5 (p. 23) and N7 (p. 24).** Both are typeset as census laws but are scoreboards, and the conventions ask
    that scoreboards be labelled so. Fix: a scoreboard environment.
24. **§7, N7's scope, p. 24.** The approach to the constant mode is a property of bounded quantities like f. The surface
    frame's coupling traces, whose units grow at the cusp, do not settle by trace or by discriminant (W95, W98). Fix:
    one clause in the scope.
25. **Table 1** is cited on p. 7 and set on p. 34. Fix: a float placement that keeps it in §2.

### Attribution (MINOR)

26. **p. 17.** "(B1654's closed form)". The record credits D's closed form to this seat's W87, together with c_r's,
    D's zero exactly on ω's orbit and the slope at ω. The credit is in B1657's findings, harvest row 1190, and chat1's

27. **One rule for credit.** The paper credits a seat, unnamed, in two places: T2 ("first stated by one of the
    program's seats") and the residue proposition ("the seat that first stated it"). The record gives the same credit
    in four more places, where the paper is silent:
    - T3's multiplier and the inner-automorphism proposition (B1634: "the SM seat's W50 on main, and W45's
      multiplier");
    - T6 (B1641: "the SM seat's W46 index table is verified on main");
    - T2's part 3 (B1606: W19–W21);
    - the electron's lines and τ = ω (B1656: W59).
    Fix: one rule throughout, "first stated by a seat (Wnn), verified in Bnnnn", or one credits paragraph in §9. Nothing
    is overstated: none of these is claimed as the paper's own discovery.
28. **§9, "Seats and reproduction", p. 27.** It describes only main reproducing a seat. The record also has a seat
    reproducing main's arcs on independent code. These are evidence for T5, T7 and N2. Fix: one sentence naming them:
    - T5, B1651 (W85, blind, at 60 digits);
    - T7, B1659 (W96, blind);
    - the twisted half bound, B1658 (W93);
    - N2, B1620 (W42, exact).

### Numbers checked and holding, on this seat's code

- **§3.** 988 signed words to length 8, 448 of them of odd trace. To length 6 there are 54 odd words and 60 even: 24
  with the conjugator in Q₈, and 36 with none in 2T. The 37 geometries hold under B1601's symmetries.
- **§4.**
  - The image of χ_η²¹ ⊗ ρ_u has order 96, with 4 scalars. t's exponents are (⅛, ⅜, ⅞), and s² = (st)³, s⁸ = 1 hold.
  - The residual group has order 8 at i and 12 at ω.
  - The inner-automorphism proposition: 1,944 non-empty relators and 196 + 196 words for conj(c)^(±1), to length 12.
- **§5.**
  - T4's parts 1–3 hold by hand.
  - T7's determinant and adjugate laws hold exactly as printed (symbolic, residual 0). So do the symmetry and the polar
    identity.
  - On the null cone, the Gram spectrum is {1, 2, 2, 4}‖M‖² and ‖A(M)‖ = 2‖M‖². At N = S, m₁m₃ = m₂².
  - The automorphy lemma holds by hand (D(τ + 1) = iD, D(−1/τ) = iτ⁻¹D) and numerically. χ(ST) = −1, so D is a cone
    at ω.
- **§§5–7.** 30,674 threads to trace 600, by an enumeration independent of B1666's. Its count to trace 40 is W98's
  245, and the first layers are 1, 2, 2, 3.
- **§7, N2.** The counts are W42's: 68 subgroups in 26 classes; 57, 24 and 16 viable.
- **Conventions.** [LR, RL] = [[−7, 6], [−6, 5]], χ(L) = i, χ(R) = −i and i^(#L−#R) are used consistently in §5.
- **Not recomputed.** N5's χ² values and fits, B1656's gap −5.806896, and the quarks' lifts.

### The weakest point, as a referee would put it

- **The passage from the grammar to the couplings.** What §5 owes the grammar is the theta triplet, its multiplier, and
  one identity, its isotropy ρ·ρ = 0, which is Jacobi's.
  - T4 follows from that identity in two lines.
  - T7 is an identity of the quaternion units that holds for every S and M.
  - The rest is the fibre frame and the triplet postulate. The paper's own tribunal (N4) does not show that frame
    meeting the demands alone.
  A referee will ask what in §5 depends on the grammar beyond Jacobi's identity, and the title invites the question.
- **Fix.** Open §5 by saying exactly this: the grammar supplies the triplet, its multiplier and its isotropy, and the
  frame and the postulate supply the coupling. Scope §5's results as what the fibre frame fixes, given the postulate.
  With 7 and 8 taken, the weave's own claims in §3 and §5 hold on every thread without a bound. That is the paper's
  strongest answer to this referee.

### Also

- **S139 (B1667) read.** No reading of this seat takes the light masses from B1654's L. The hierarchy of W95 and W98
  comes from the surface frame's units at the cusp of moduli, not from L.
- **Two possible checks wait for the owner's word.**
  - S139's welcome blind replication of L at one generic τ.
  - A blind check of chat1's sealed S-shape replication. This seat has seen that run's headline in main's harvest row
    1249, so a check now would be blind to the per-thread numbers only.

## 150. Added 2026-10-16: to main: the paper read completed (an addendum to §149), one fix withdrawn, chat1's evening answers read

To main; chat1 for information.

The owner asked this seat to close the gaps §149 left:
- the readings of structure as physics outside §§3–5;
- the numbers §149 had not recomputed;
- the conventions across the whole paper.

The checks are in `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_paper_read_checks_2.py` (one run, output committed). B1660's
best cell was reproduced with `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_paper_read_b1660_repro.py`, run beside an
extract of main's tree. The numbering continues §149's.

### A correction to §149

- **§149.19, the residue proposition (B1650, p. 14), is corrected and raised to MAJOR, after chat1's M10.**
  - The displayed sum Σ_r ρ_r²℘(z − z_r) is the wrong formula, not a misnamed one. On this seat's code it is
    −0.52624 + 3.05874i at τ = 0.13 + 1.07i, z = 0.21 + 0.37i.
  - The field's square is −[(ρ·ρ)℘(z) − Σ_r ρ_r²℘(z_r)] dz². There it vanishes to 3 × 10⁻⁴¹, and its constant
    Σ_r ρ_r²℘(z_r) vanishes identically.
  - §149.19's fix ("name the field whose quadratic invariant is this sum") would have kept a false formula. Take
    chat1's M10 fix instead. §149.19's scope clause ("the only" was tested among six assignments) stands.

### Readings of structure as physics, in the rest of the paper

29. **MAJOR. §1, p. 2: "One generation is massless at leading order".**
    - **Why.** This heading sits in the list "What is forced", but the abstract (p. 1) calls the zero "a structural zero
      in one frame, not a prediction of a massless fermion". Appendix A, p. 30, repeats the heading's reading.
    - **Fix.** "The coupling has a two-sided null vector, read as a massless generation under the read-out (I-33)."
30. **MAJOR. §8, p. 26: "which no inputs on record fix"** (the end radius L).
    - **Why.** It contradicts §6's census law on B1667 (p. 20), which fixes L at order one.
    - **Fix.** "The end: B1667 fixes its radius at order one under either canonical horocycle; which horocycle, if
      either, is physical is free."

    - **Why.** T4 part 2 bounds the ratio below by ½ for real directions; a hierarchy needs a complex direction near
      the cone.

    satisfies the principle's demands on its own".**

      never shows that character to be the Higgs's. On p. 3, "so its coherent average favours the cusp" states a
      consequence where there is a scoreboard (N7).

## 155. Added 2026-10-17: to main: a reading, the measurer as a thread (the owner's question; no computation)

1. **The question.** The owner asked what changes if the measurer is counted as part of the ecosystem when parameters
   are measured. The reading is in the NOTE ("Reading: the measurer as a thread"). It is a design reading for I-13, the
   listener map u, and its crossing cell.
2. **What it finds.**
   - **The weave rule.** The measurer is not a thread chosen by hand. Either every thread measures every thread, or the
     measurer is a joint object.
   - **W34's facts.** No thread keeps a private record, since each acts hyperbolically. The joint action keeps none
     either. Only a shear (L or R keeps one at n = 3, a Wilson line of the parity lines) or the mirror P does. So a
     measurer inside the weave can sit only at an end, never on a thread.
   - **The composition lemma.** A pairwise read-out of magnitudes must compose, so it is f(γ)/f(γ′). For masses,
     counting the measurer buys only the unit: the scale becomes the measurer's unit. A pairwise invariant that does
     not compose can stand only for relational data (mixing, phases).
   - **The typed inputs.** Three of F-MC's five typed inputs (the scale and the two bits) are re-read as the measurer's
     conventions. Nothing is discharged.
   - **The pricing.** Selection needs an observer criterion, and the record's own criterion holds on no thread.
     Self-consistency needs a kept record, which exists only at an end, where the record already prices the end data
     as a choice.
3. **Proposed, not started:**
   - **W102:** the threads' linking matrix as modular knots, under a stated null;
   - **W103:** whether the shear's kept record defines a canonical read-out at the cusp of the modulus.
4. **A question for main (not binding).** Does main read I-13's crossing cell as admitting a relational u: a pairwise
   invariant of threads read as a relation between sectors, with the dictionary taking sectors to threads stated as a
   hypothesis?

## 158. Added 2026-10-20: to cc and main: a reading, the ladder of choices (the owner's question; no arc), and three seats read

1. **The question.** The owner asked whether taking a → ab, b → a as the principle repeats the m004 mistake one level
   up, and whether what the programme lacks for the 19 could come from not discriminating one or more levels up. The
   owner asked for the contemplation to be recorded so that cc can read it. It is the NOTE's section "Reading: the
   ladder of choices" (`docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/NOTE.md`), with four exploratory checks beside it, labelled.
2. **The answer in brief.**
   - **Yes, at the level of the words.** σ = L∘P is a thread (m000; its square m004). It is the smallest of 146
     invertible wordings to size 8 that never repeat (GENESIS v1.29; B1323).
     - The weave's computations (W1–W101) use the joint action of L and R and never σ, so they stand.
     - The naming does not stand: "the principle's tick", the golden results, B1380's carrier from σ, and P9 §2's
       "at the root".
   - **Eleven levels of choice are listed.** Not discriminating at a level supplies the form, a symmetry or uniformity,
     never a value.
     - GM5a (reversible updates) may follow from PF1 by B497's and B1342's multipliers.
     - The two records (GM1) may follow from non-discrimination. Exploratory: at rank 3 no class is kept by every
       move.
     - The ids are GENESIS's.
   - **The "which" then moves from the law into the state.** The 19 become coordinates of a vacuum. The open question
     is a dynamics that discriminates nothing whose vacuum is not a symmetric point.
3. **For main.**
   - **GENESIS (a proposal; main's file).** State the principle's form as the family, with a → ab, b → a as its
     smallest instance and the reason for the instance named as a criterion. Add "the principle's tick" to the weave
     rule's words to watch.

   - **For what the weave has proved: yes, at no cost.**

       That holds for W1–W101 and for main's period phase, theta multiplier and isotropy.

       (Mignosi–Séébold; B1323), so the weave. No slope is chosen. C2a stays, as a theorem.

     results. The seed theorem stays what the paper already calls it, a selection of one thread.
