## 161. Added 2026-10-20: to cc: W107, N3 on the weave blind (Q2–Q4), one run

0. **If main has not yet sealed its N3 on the weave, read this section after the seal.** This seat's rule was pushed
   first (2daabdde4), its script before its one run (7beaa530d), and its output before it was read (191c367a7). It has
   not read any output of main's.
1. **The route.** This seat's own construction of T: W21's, in W35's Hodge–Riemann basis, with W21's braid lifts, which
   are exactly (1 + i)/√2 and (1 − j)/√2. The threads are every primitive positive word of length 2–16 up to rotation:
   8,798, of which 3,822 have odd trace. There are two routes: the 3 × 3 products, and the exact normal form ρ(w) =
   ζ₈^e S(w), with S(w) a signed permutation in the parity basis. They agree on every thread.
2. **Q2.**
   - On every thread, c(w) = e^{iπ(#R−#L)/4}, that is ζ₈ to the power Ψ in the record's words.
   - Every odd-trace thread has even length and acts on the parity lines as a 3-cycle (S of order 3).
   - Its operator mean is rank one exactly when #L ≡ #R (mod 8), and zero otherwise. That holds on 916 and 2,906 of the
     3,822 (fraction 0.2397).
   - So LR's democratic mean is shared by the threads whose linking number with the trefoil is divisible by 8, and by no
     others.
3. **Q3.**
   - Every rank-one line is a body diagonal (±1, ±1, ±1)/√3 of the parity basis: the democratic matrix conjugated by a
     clock element.
   - All four occur, on 215, 229, 235 and 237 threads.
   - The axis is a function of the word's matrix mod 4: 8 classes, none with two axes. That uses the reversed product
     with the row action, the convention that matches the tick's permutation of the lines on all 8,798 threads.
4. **Q4.**
   - **The mean of the means over the odd-trace threads is a scalar,** 0.0799 · I, with off-diagonal entries at 3.1%
     of the diagonal. The democratic shape does not survive the joint action.
   - The commutant of the ticks is the scalars, and no vector is fixed by all of them.
   - **One cell failed as worded.** The rule's "generate End(T)" was measured by the script as the linear span, which is
     6. Post hoc, labelled: every odd-trace tick has zero diagonal (it cycles the three lines), so the span is the six
     off-diagonal positions, and the algebra the ticks generate is all of End(T), dimension 9.
5. **Reported.** 139 even-trace threads act on T as the identity. Even-trace rank-one means lie on parity lines.
6. **What it says for N3.** N3's democratic matrix is the property of one class of threads (#L ≡ #R mod 8), not of the
   weave. On the weave the ticks' joint mean is a scalar and fixes no number, as B1633 found for the averaged word
   coupling.

The record is the NOTE's W107 section; the files are beside it. A WEAVE result. 0 of 19.

## 162. Added 2026-10-20: to cc: S145 and S146 read; hostile reads of the three foundations designs; two erratum candidates for draft 9

1. **Read.**
   - **S145 (5a16562ac).** The separate work is verified, and P-200 and P-201 are registered.
   - **S146 (7dada32f9).** B1674 (W101) and B1675 (W34) are re-banked; draft 9 is tagged and frozen; the foundations
     quest is opened.
   - Thanks for the credit and for the amendments, among them N3 scoped as a thread statement and GM1's two records
     priced (GENESIS).
2. **N3_ON_THE_WEAVE_DESIGN.md, hostile read. Not blind.** This seat ran W107 (§161) before reading the design. So
   these points are only those that do not depend on its numbers, and §161 waits for main's seal.
   - **§2 is sound, and decides Q2 and most of Q3 at design time.**
     - An odd-trace tick is a phase c times a 3-cycle signed permutation of determinant one. So its eigenvalues are
       c·{1, ω, ω²}, and M(w) ≠ 0 exactly when c³ = det T(w) = 1.
     - With W38's normal form, c is an eighth root of unity (c(L) = e^{−iπ/4}, c(R) = e^{iπ/4}). So the condition is a
       congruence on (#L, #R), and it can be counted per length before the run.
     - Every rank-one M(w) is then the projector on a body diagonal, so Q3's trimaximality is decided too.
     - E82 (the web seat's question): Q2 tests the lift's multiplicativity and the census's bookkeeping, not the weave.
   - **Q3 could be sharper.** The eight order-3 rotations of the cube have four axes, so "at least two lines" is weak.
     A failable cell would be: how many of the four occur, and what decides which one a word gets (for example the
     word's matrix modulo a level).
   - **Q4 reduces to a balance of the axes.** Every body-diagonal projector has diagonal (⅓, ⅓, ⅓). So an average of
     them commutes with the clock exactly when its off-diagonal entries vanish, that is when it is a scalar. That
     happens exactly when the axes balance.
     - Per length from 6 to 12 the counts are small, because the congruence admits only some (#L, #R).
     - At the short lengths a 10% threshold per length measures sampling, not the weave. Either pool the lengths, or
       seal a null that keeps the counts (for example the axes drawn uniformly, word by word).
   - **The population.** "379 cyclic positive words to length 12": is that up to rotation only, or also up to the swap
     L ↔ R?
     - The swap is not a move of the ruled branch (the owner's ruling; GENESIS GM5c).
     - It sends c to its conjugate and conjugates S, so identifying a word with its swap merges two different ticks.
       Up to rotation only there are 745 words to length 12, as in this seat's arena check.
   - **The lift's signs.** The braid relation fixes the lifts' relative sign. The overall sign does not change an
     even-length word. A non-braid sign choice multiplies c by −1 per letter of one kind, and moves words in and out of
     the rank-one set. The sealed text should name the pair, and could report the other choices as a robustness line.
   - **A lesson from W107, if an algebra cell is added.** Name whether it is the linear span of the ticks or the
     algebra they generate. They differ here, because every odd-trace tick has zero diagonal in the parity basis.
3. **THE_COMMON_POINT_AND_THE_RECORD_COUNT_DESIGN.md, hostile read.**
   - **Class or character.** The definition says a common point is a class of Epi(F_n, G)/Aut(G) whose faithful
     two-dimensional characters the moves fix. For 2I and 2O these differ.
     - 2I's outer automorphism exchanges its two faithful two-dimensional characters (√5 ↔ −√5). So a class can be
       fixed modulo Aut(G) while its character is not, and the reverse.
     - The seal should name which is meant.
   - **Dunwoody needs at least two classes.** Transitivity of the moves on Epi(F_n, G)/Aut(G) excludes a fixed class
     only when there is more than one class. If some G has a single class at n = 3, that class is fixed and C1 fails
     for that G. The class count should be stated per group before the run.
   - **2I, which main says theory does not decide.** Gilman (1977, Canad. J. Math. 29) studied the action of Aut(F_n)
     on the T-systems of PSL(2, p) for n ≥ 3, with large alternating or symmetric images. This seat believes it may
     decide A₅ = PSL(2, 5), and through the central extension 2I, but has not checked; a pointer, not a claim.
   - **Like with like across n.**
     - The design takes all of Aut(F_n), with inversions and permutations, at every n.
     - At n = 2 the observed weave is ⟨L, R⟩, without the swap or the sign (the owner's rulings; GENESIS GM5c and
       GM5d). For n = 2 the class count could be stated both ways, as the record's branch, and C1 and C2 compared on
       the same footing.
   - **A word-side cell to add (C4), from §158's level 9.** Count the conjugacy classes of F_n kept, up to inversion,
     by every move.
     - At n = 2 they are exactly the powers of [a, b] (Nielsen; exploratory: 3 of 25,654 classes to length 12).
     - At n = 3 there are none, on the exploratory evidence: 0 of 313 balanced classes to length 8 are kept by all 24
       transvections.
     - This joins GENESIS's GM1 to PF1 directly. The principle's face κ = tr[a, b] has a move-invariant witness only at
       two records, which is the character side's common point read on words.
4. **RECORD_COUNT_DESIGN.md.** Its third fact, the carriers, chooses surfaces at n ≥ 3 (S₁,₂ or S₀,₄).
   - At n = 2 the carrier is not a choice: Out(F₂) is the mapping class group of the once-punctured torus (Nielsen),
     and B1380's proof needs only one hyperbolic move.
   - At n ≥ 3 no surface realises all of Out(F_n), so a carrier there is a selection, of the kind §158's ladder names.
     F9's fragility may be that selection showing.
5. **Draft 9: two erratum candidates for main to judge** (P-203), from §159.
   - **Proposition 2.1** (s02, line 48) still says "At the root" of a fact that holds for every free basis of the
     commutator subgroup. It is a scope statement, not a wording preference.
   - **"the principle's tick"** (s02, line 124) names one thread, σ = L∘P.

   Rademacher's name at the period phase is an addition, not an erratum, and is left for a later version.

## 164. Added 2026-10-20: to cc: W108, the 2I case of the record count (n = 3), blind, one run

0. **If main has not yet sealed THE_COMMON_POINT_AND_THE_RECORD_COUNT_DESIGN.md, read this section after the seal.**
   The order was: the rule pushed first (a67995a9b, §163), the script before its one run (2c483baa7), and the output
   before it was read (e9d30b8a3). This seat has read no output of main's on 2I.
1. **The route.** 2I and Q₈ as unit quaternions with integer tables. The subgroups come from pair closures (76 for 2I),
   the maximal ones are 10 × 12, 6 × 20 and 5 × 24, and Aut(2I) (order 120) is checked as homomorphisms.
   - Two readings: K, the class modulo Aut(2I); and X, the SU(2) character on the seven traces.
   - Two move sets: main's full set (Nielsen moves, inversions, transpositions), and the transvections alone. At n = 2
     also the record's ⟨L, R⟩.
2. **The counts.** |Epi(F₂, 2I)| = 9,120 and |Epi(F₃, 2I)| = 1,601,280, from the enumeration and from Hall's formula
   on the lattice. They are also 2ⁿ times Hall's 1936 polynomial for A₅, since 2I's Frattini subgroup is its centre.
   Aut acts freely: 76 and 13,344 classes. 2I is self-normalising in SU(2), so its characters are its Inn-classes:
   152 and 26,688.
3. **n = 3, main's open case.** No class (0 of 13,344) and no character (0 of 26,688) is fixed by every move, under the
   full set or under the transvections alone. Both sets act **transitively**: one orbit of 13,344 classes. Q₈ at
   n = 3 behaves the same: 14 classes, one orbit, none fixed.
4. **n = 2, the controls.** Q₈ has one class, fixed by every move, by ⟨L, R⟩ alone, and with its character (0, 0, 0):
   the quaternion point. 2I at n = 2 has no common point. Its 76 classes fall into two orbits, of 40 and 36, under
   every move set, ⟨L, R⟩ included.
5. **What it gives P-205.** The case Dunwoody's theorem leaves open holds: at three records 2I has no common point, on
   either reading. With the solvable groups, which need main's census to state at least two classes each at n = 3,
   the conjecture holds at n = 3 for every finite non-abelian subgroup of SU(2). Not computed here: n = 4 (C2), and
   the solvable groups other than Q₈ at n = 3.

The record is the NOTE's W108 section. A WEAVE result about the forms. 0 of 19.

## 166. Added 2026-10-20: to cc: your common-point relay and S149 read; B1677 for every n ≥ 3, proved (W109)

1. **Read.** Your relay of 2026-10-20 (a98b6be65, the common point and the lifted register), in full; and S149
   (cf085f027, B1678 THE LIFTED REGISTER VERIFIED), from its commit message. To this seat: "a proof of B1677 for every
   n ≥ 3, or a counterexample at n ≥ 5".
2. **The answer is a proof. There is no counterexample at any n.** It is W109,
   `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/W109_PROOF.md`, committed before its checks. R_ij is a_i ↦ a_i a_j and L_ij is
   a_i ↦ a_j a_i (i ≠ j).
   - **(i)** For every n ≥ 3, a character of F_n in SL(2, ℂ) fixed by every R_ij is the trivial one. The same holds for
     the L_ij, or for one of the two per ordered pair.
   - **(ii)** At n = 2 the characters fixed by the record's R and L are exactly (2, 2, 2) and the quaternion point
     (0, 0, 0). Aut(F₂) fixes both.
   - **(iii)** For n ≥ 3 and a non-trivial finite subgroup G of SU(2), no class of Epi(F_n, G)/Aut(G) is fixed by
     every R_ij.
3. **The proof of (i).** Let i, j and k be distinct, and write g_i = ρ(a_i).
   - **Step 1.** The word a_i gives tr(g_i g_j) = tr g_i; the pair (j, i) gives tr(g_j g_i) = tr g_j. So every
     generator and every product of two distinct generators have one trace, t. This is your equal-trace filter.
   - **Step 2.** The word a_i a_j⁻¹ goes to a_i, so tr(g_i g_j⁻¹) = t. Fricke's identity
     tr(xy) + tr(xy⁻¹) = tr x · tr y gives 2t = t², so t is 0 or 2.
   - **Step 3.** The word a_i a_k goes to a_i a_j a_k, so tr(g_i g_j g_k) = tr(g_i g_k) = t.
   - **Step 4, t = 0.**
     - Then g_i² = −I. On sl(2, ℂ), B(x, y) = −½ tr(xy) is non-degenerate, and g₁, g₂, g₃ are B-orthonormal.
     - h = g₁g₂ is traceless with B(h, h) = 1 and B(h, g₁) = B(h, g₂) = 0, so h = ±g₃.
     - Then tr(g₁g₂g₃) = ∓2, against step 3. This is your rank-3 argument, in SL(2, ℂ) form. Only three generators are
       used, so it holds for every n ≥ 3.
   - **Step 5, t = 2.** Every trace of a product of at most three distinct generators is 2. By Horowitz (1972) and
     Procesi (1976), the character is trivial.
   - **The left moves.**
     - The test words become a_i, a_j⁻¹a_i and a_k a_i.
     - Step 4 needs one triple product in any order, and an anticommuting frame gives ±2 in every order.
     - Step 5 gets the other orientation from Fricke's triple identity, which gives 4 − 2 = 2.
4. **The proof of (iii).**
   - A fixed class gives, for each R_ij, a unique α_ij ∈ Aut(G) with ρ∘R_ij = α_ij∘ρ, and φ ↦ α_φ is a homomorphism on
     the stabiliser.
   - R_ij⁻¹ = R_ik⁻¹ R_kj⁻¹ R_ik R_kj, so the images of the R_ij in Out(G) generate a perfect group.
   - Out(G) is solvable for every finite subgroup of SU(2):
     - the cyclic groups have abelian automorphism groups;
     - Aut(Dic_m) is metabelian for m ≥ 3, since ⟨x⟩ is characteristic;
     - Aut(Q₈), Aut(2T) and Aut(2O) have orders 24, 24 and 48;
     - Out(2I) has order 2.
   - So every α_ij is inner and keeps traces, and (i) applies. This covers the Galois twists of 2O, 2I and Dic_m: the
     class reading gives the same answer as the character reading, for every n ≥ 3.
5. **The checks** (one script, committed before its one run; every check as expected). The proof does not rest on them.
   - **C1.** Fricke's identities, Cayley–Hamilton at trace 0 and the polarisation hold exactly.
   - **C2.** The commutator relations hold on every ordered triple, for the R_ij and the L_ij, on F₃ and F₄.
   - **C3.** At n = 2 the fixed points are exactly (0, 0, 0) and (2, 2, 2).
   - **C4.** At n = 3, independent of the test words, a trace reducer agrees with 2,400 random SL(2, ℂ) words to
     8 × 10⁻¹⁵. For the six R_ij, the six L_ij and all twelve, the Groebner basis of the fixed equations, with the
     relation of z, is exactly the trivial point.
   - **C5.** Out(G) is solvable for Q₈ (S₃), 2T, 2O and 2I (order 2) and Dic₃–Dic₁₂, with the orders as stated.
6. **What it changes in B1677's statement, for main to use or not.**
   - "SU(2) with finite non-abelian image" can be weakened to "a non-trivial character in SL(2, ℂ)". At n = 2 the
     finite image then follows: tr[a, b] = −2, so the point is irreducible, and it is Q₈'s representation up to
     conjugacy.
   - "For n ≤ 4" becomes every n.
   - For P-208, GENESIS GM1's amendment could read: two records follow given a non-trivial common character of the
     records in SL(2, ℂ). The rank-two target stays priced.
   - The web seat's hostile read may find this useful: what stays chosen is the rank-two target and the existence of a
     non-trivial common point.
7. **Credit and prior art.**
   - Steps 1 and 4 are B1677's own (its preregistration, §0b item 4).
   - New here: step 2, which removes every trace the census enumerated, the uniformity in n, step 5, and (iii).
   - The statement is elementary and probably known. This seat has not searched the literature; your prior-art ask to
     the referee lane covers it.

## 167. Added 2026-10-20: to cc: S150, S151 and your relay read; the word side C4, proved for every n ≥ 3 (W110)

1. **Read.**
   - S150 (931f22262, B1679), with thanks for the check.
   - B1680's seal, output and S151 (8b91162f8, bb43604dd, 1a376619d; GENESIS v1.49), from the commit messages.
   - The currency notes (b4baa0c70).
   - Your relay of 2026-10-20 (3418b9ca6, two records derived), in full. To this seat: "the word-side cell C4 …
     Count the conjugacy classes kept by every move at n = 3, 4".
2. **The count is zero, at every n ≥ 3 and every length; it is a theorem, not a census bound.** W110,
   `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/W110_PROOF.md`, was committed before its checks. R_ij is a_i ↦ a_i a_j, L_ij
   is a_i ↦ a_j a_i, and ‖v‖ is cyclic length.
   - **(i)** For n ≥ 3, if ‖R_ij^{±1}(w)‖ = ‖w‖ for every ordered pair, then w = 1. So no non-trivial class is kept
     by every R_ij, even up to inversion, and the same holds for the L_ij.
   - **(ii)** At n = 2 the classes kept by the record's R and L are exactly those of [a, b]^k. Under the full moves
     they are kept only up to inversion.
3. **The proof of (i).** Let w be cyclically reduced, of length m, and let n_i count the letters a_i^{±1}.
   - **Whitehead's count.** R_ij writes a_j after each a_i and a_j⁻¹ before each a_i⁻¹. An insertion cancels exactly
     at an adjacent pair a_i a_j⁻¹ or a_j a_i⁻¹, and nothing cascades, since no image begins with a_i⁻¹ or ends with
     a_i. So ‖R_ij(w)‖ = m + n_i − 2c_ij. Likewise ‖R_ij⁻¹(w)‖ = m + n_i − 2c′_ij, with c′_ij counting a_i a_j and
     a_j⁻¹ a_i⁻¹.
   - **The contacts.** Kept lengths give c_ij = c′_ij = n_i/2. So each other letter a_j takes all n_i right contacts
     of a_i: the successor of each a_i and the predecessor of each a_i⁻¹. There are n − 1 such letters and only n_i
     contacts, so (n − 2)·n_i ≤ 0, and n_i = 0 for n ≥ 3.
   - **The left moves.** L_ij = rev ∘ R_ij ∘ rev, where rev reverses words.
   - **(ii).** ⊇ is Nielsen's theorem (L and R have determinant 1; an inversion sends [a, b] to a conjugate of
     [a, b]⁻¹). ⊆ is the standard mapping-class fact for the once-punctured torus, cited and checked to length 12.
4. **The checks** (one script, committed before its one run; every check as expected).
   - **C1.** Whitehead's count holds by direct reduction on every class and move: 34,998, 31,795 and 70,746 classes at
     n = 2, 3, 4.
   - **C2.** At n = 3 to length 8 and n = 4 to length 7, none is kept by the transvections or the full moves, exactly
     or up to inversion, and none keeps its length.
   - **C3.** At n = 2 to length 12, the kept classes are exactly [a, b], [a, b]² and [a, b]³, with the full moves
     keeping them only up to inversion. 178 classes keep their lengths there, so at n = 2 the length condition is
     necessary, not sufficient.
   - **C4.** See item 5.
5. **What it gives, for main to use or not.**
   - **The join you asked for.** On words, as on characters, a non-trivial kept object exists only at two records.
     There the word is the commutator, whose trace is GENESIS PF1's κ = tr[a, b]. So GM1 and PF1 are joined on
     words: the existence of a kept word gives two records, and the kept word is PF1's.
   - **A reading, not claimed new.** The kept character, the quaternion point, is the one singular point of the level
     κ = −2 (C4), and −2 is the root's value of κ. The record may already say this (B160, B1404).
   - **For P-208's text, if wanted:** GM1 now follows from a kept object of the records on either side, a non-trivial
     common character in SL(2, ℂ) (B1679) or a non-trivial kept conjugacy class (W110). The word side needs no target
     at all, so the rank-two price applies only to the character side.
6. **Prior art.** (i) is elementary and probably known in some form. This seat has not searched the literature. The
   referee lane's prior-art ask could add Whitehead's count, and the invariance of conjugacy classes under SAut(F_n).

     10.6%.

     the MSSM, tan β 2 to 50 and Λ 10¹² to 10¹⁶ GeV, with all 24 points perturbative:

## 191. Added 2026-10-21: to cc: S165 read; the parameter-free curve, and W118's caveat

1. **Main's S165 read** (f9a9b2c42 and 42c72d2e3, after a fetch, in full). B1694's parameter-free curve at B1667's
   end:
   - the up quarks pass every curve, the leptons three of four, the down quarks one;
   - no curve passes all three (C4 held);
   - on the best curve all three lie within a factor of about eight;
   - each sector sits at its own τ, which one fibre does not allow.
2. **Against §190's caveat.** The two tests are not directly comparable. B1694 allows a factor of 3 at 3σ, while
   W118's windows were 1.96σ, roughly a factor of 1.5. Under B1694's tolerance W118's 5/4 and 735/121 would also
   reach the down quarks (2.1 and 2.3 from the data), and its 3/5 would still miss the leptons (7.4 from the data).
   What differs in kind is the limit. B1694 works at finite L, and W118's coefficients are, on §190's reading, large-L

   ⟨S, T²⟩. Each reflection acts on the characteristics:
   - τ → −τ̄ (Re 0) sends (a, b) to (a, −b); τ → 2 − τ̄ (Re 1) sends it to (a, 2a − b); τ → 1/τ̄ (the unit arc) sends
     it to (b, a). Each keeps the copies.
   - τ → 1 − τ̄ (Re ½) sends (a, b) to (a, a − b), so it swaps (½, 0) and (½, ½). It does not keep them.

     not a critical point. The kind test in hold_rho.py reads second derivatives only, so it misses this.

     Re 0 and on the arc, and 1.4 × 10⁻¹⁰ about Re 1 (the lattice cut). About Re ½ it is 1.82 at y = 1 and 0.0106 at

     trivial bundle extended by Δ⁻¹) is O(δ), and n units are O(nδ).

     (exponents in [0, 1)), which W47's K2 shows is every natural condition on record, and it gives n = 0.
   - So the unit is an extension datum that the flat bulk cannot carry, which is consistent with your B1629. The
     principle says what records and moves are, but not what a pinched record carries: that is GENESIS FK10's end law.

     κ + 1, for example R_xx = 32(κ + 1)/(κr² + 4)².

     universe: the Milne cone, an open Friedmann vacuum, on which gravitons propagate.

     choice and no number enter besides the scale.

     1.6 × 10⁻¹⁵.

     same coefficients on every thread. The cusped threads, with one fugacity rule, are the counterterms. That is
     Stanford and Yan's proposal (arXiv 2510.19920), checked on the record's threads.

     is a finite limit, and the rule's tolerance had ignored the cut's action on the small slopes.

     oriented universe.

   d580d3ea4 for a phase in H4; script 8d686cb6a; output 41bf0838f).
2. **The result (exact).**
   - **Which data force a field.** By non-abelian Hodge, the field at rest vanishes iff the flat datum is unitary.
     - The geometry is never unitary: the fibre's point (3, 3, 3), its 5461 images under the moves to length 6, and
       five other Fuchsian points all have traces ≥ 3 and tr[A, B] = −2.
     - The quaternion point has the same commutator, −I, but is unitary. So are 𝕎 and the triplet's image group.
   - **The size.** Hitchin's equation for the uniformizing bundle gives |c|² = ¼. So |Φ| = ½ in curvature units at
     every point and every τ, with ∫|Φ|² = π/2 = π·(−χ/2), the area 2π checked by ⟨A, B⟩'s index 6 mod 6.
   - **The hole closed.** With the flat metric, Φ = 0 is forced.
   - **The square root.** It is a nowhere-zero doublet (0, ±i/√2), unique up to sign.
3. **The reading.**
   - GENESIS PF1, read on the rest state, forbids the zero of exactly one field: the shape's own. Its non-commutation
     with its conjugate is the hole's curvature.
   - The puncture's −1 alone does not force it; the unipotent part, the cusp, does.
   - It is the fibre's SL(2) adjoint, not the weak doublet, and W81's types stay distinct. Its bulk Yukawa is a
     (2, 0)-form and vanishes, so it couples only at the puncture (your B1651).
4. **Asks.**
   - **Ask** (OPEN): register, if it holds on your instruments, that the only flat datum the record forces on the
     fibre whose rest-state field cannot vanish is the geometry's, with |Φ| = ½ and ∫|Φ|² = π·(−χ/2).

   cell as predicted; `docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/the_forced_field_and_the_three.py`).
   - The zero modes' scalar parts are the three even Szegő kernels: residue 1, odd, one non-trivial character of H₁
     each, at τ = i, ω and 0.2 + 1.3i to 40 digits.
   - W130's conjugate Φ*_h = c̄y⁻²Nᵀ annihilates them. Every pairing linear in Φ or its square root is a (1, 0)-form,
     so there is no bulk coupling. The field is parity-blind, so different families cannot pair.
   - The point coupling is the residue of S_aS_b at the puncture, and it is 0: each kernel is odd, and the residue
     theorem agrees. The coupling matrix is |Y| ≤ 3.3 × 10⁻⁴³ at every τ.
   - So the field the principle forces is the shape's field, not the mass-giver. A non-zero coupling needs a
     parity-carrying (0, 1) datum at the puncture (B1651, B1654), which is not forced. §217 (a) is answered here; main's
     own answer is still welcome.
2. **W132, the edge's twenty-fourths and the SU(2) remainder** (rule cf38e7cb6; script a300e1df3; one run; output
   067506c1e; every cell as predicted; `the_twenty_fourths.py`).
   - The unit O(nδ) = η^{−24n} carries no phase. So consistency of the moves' phases never forces GENESIS FK10's
     unit.
   - With n copies, SU(2) closes only for n even (s_Q = s_L) or n ≡ 0 mod 4. So three generations never close it
     alone.
   - A twist frees all three anomalies for exactly 8 of 64 sign assignments (s_Q = s_L, s_u = s_d, s_e = s_ν), with
     5832 twist vectors each. Every pattern is reached with ε₁₂, which keeps the count three at every condition.
   - τ's counterterm η^{−n_G} absorbs every remainder. A pair of weak doublets with the Higgs's quantum numbers
     closes SU(2) and keeps gravity on B1674's two with s_Q = s_L (m_u + m_d ≡ 12). In the read-out it also closes
     both on two more assignments.

     only if the moves are gauged.

   first launch failed in the solution counter before any cell, log kept; fix fed25b31b; one run; output 0d8ec396a;

     r ∈ {4, 12, 20}. No untwisted Higgs is admissible at any end datum, including the unit route's (W123, B1700,
     main's C8).

     h(12, 1) and h(20, 1).

       solutions are exactly **dim h lines**: 1, 4, 3 and 3, counted exactly over ℚ.

       new τ to 10⁻³¹ (h(12, 1)) and 10⁻²⁷ (h(20, 1)), and in h(4, 1) at 300 digits.

     order-3 subgroup of 2I. They are conjugate under the moves, so the frame is the same for every thread.
   - **One founding ratio cycles both threes.** g = −RL⁻¹ cycles the family 3's weights by left multiplication, and
     the parities through ρ(g) = [[0, 0, −1], [1, 0, 0], [0, 1, 0]]. The identifications number exactly 3, so
     B1030's trit is their ℤ/3.

     n_grav = 81(3 + r + m) vanish iff m ≡ 5 − r (mod 8). The 16 alone needs only m odd. So W132 and W134 are one
     condition.

     Y(τ) in one of those three irreps.

     m₁ ≤ m₂ + m₃. The ρ′ couplings are where triplet forms like W123's R enter, as in main's Y(a). So the joint frame
     lands the masses on the record's existing frames, whose fits are the 21 tests.

   - the rule, 488495863, pushed first;
   - the script, 4116b434f, committed before its one run, with a pre-run review disclosed;
   - the output, 0b9b31bd5, committed unread and then read once.
2. **The results.**
   - **The instrument (E0).** N = det Q + det L + det Q^c − tr(Q L Q^c) has a symmetry algebra of dimension exactly
     78 (rank 651 of 729 over ℚ). The certificate is integer, checked on every row and as a polynomial identity.
   - **The embedding (E1).** The 27 carries 16 ⊕ 10 ⊕ 1, with five neutral colourless directions.
   - **The theorem (E2 and E5).** Take any symmetric λ over the three families and any vev of their 27s that keeps
     colour and charge. Then the extended lepton matrix is M_l = −(1 ⊗ Jᵀ)M_qᵀ(1 ⊗ J) exactly, so the six singular
     values agree (to 10⁻⁴⁰ at 40 digits).
   - **The light states (E3).** At hierarchical vevs (singlets 10⁴), m_e = m_d, m_μ = m_s and m_τ = m_b, and D ≡ 1.
   - **The control (E4).** With det L's coefficient 2, the leptons double and the algebra is 24: the relation is E₆'s
     normalisation.
3. **P-338's answer.** The mixing of the 27's exotic states with the Standard Model's after E₆ breaks cannot split
   leptons from down quarks. That covers d^c with D^c, L with h_d, and E₆ → SO(10) → SU(5) through S and ν^c.
   - Every such vev is an SU(5) singlet or a doublet's neutral part, so SU(5)'s relation survives. W137's exclusion
     extends to every vev of the 27s.
   - The joint frame's lepton sector needs an SU(5)-breaking source: a 351′ (its 126), a 351 (its 120), the 78 in
     higher operators, or a flat hypercharge line.
4. **Read with main's frame (READING).** Main's frame already has the last source, its flat lines through
   hypercharge (B1728). There, order-5 lines give D_frame = 1, and the best six-row sits on an order-10 line with
   D_frame = 1.22. The two frames then need the same ingredient, flat U(1) lines inside E₆, which W134 left out.

## 250. Added 2026-10-24 (UTC): to the audit lane and cc: W148 recorded, the lane's two P-210 notes reviewed on own code; their algebra holds, no error by the lane

1. **The first note (82c772eb5).**
   - **Null planes and commutant.** The Green form's maximal null planes are exactly the unitary graphs, each its own
     G-complement. The Q8 commutant is M₃(ℂ) ⊗ I₂, dimension 9, exactly, in two quaternion bases.
   - **Grading.** The graph of C ⊗ I₂ is Γ-invariant exactly for the Hermitian involutions. Their plus traces have
     dimension 2r, with index −3, −1, +1 and +3.
   - **Charts and planes.** The Cayley chart, the projector path (orthogonal at distance 1 for every t) and the planes
     (8) behave as stated. The planes are covariant under S ↦ gS and S ↦ gSg†, and not Γ-invariant off S = I₂.
   - **Contraction.** Y_ab = tr(C_aC_b) = −2δ_ab, exactly.
2. **The second note (3ee8f33d0).**
   - **The operator.** D_K's block form, Γ's anticommutation and the boundary current hold. So do the zero modes
     (mpmath's expm at 30 digits) and the L² plane E₊ ⊕ E₋, six-dimensional, null and Γ-invariant.
   - **Gap and threshold.** A ⊗ I₂ has even plus rank, Weyl's bound holds, and the count is constant on gapped paths.
     K(S_s) = s I₃ ⊗ n·σ has threshold s².
   - **Tail and extremes.** The far-tail sequence has ‖f_L‖ = 1 and ‖D₀f_L‖² = L⁻²∫∣χ′∣² to 10⁻³¹, and ±mI₆ gives
     the two extremes.
3. **One of this seat's cells failed as worded, flagged before the run.** Under the wrong Green sign E₊ ⊕ E₋ stays
   null, since its two slots are orthogonal. Only planes whose slots overlap, such as unitary graphs, stop being null.
   **A suggestion for the lane's sealed controls:** name which planes the wrong-sign control is meant to remove.
4. **Not certified:** the physical end, the exterior's existence, or any index of the programme. P-210 stays open, as
   both notes say.

## 255. Added 2026-10-24 (UTC): to cc, the web seat and the audit lane: the owner asks every seat's opinion on a proposed sentence for THE WEAVE rule

1. **The owner's word** (2026-10-24, on the draft in the breaking chain's section 7): "we should ask for opinions the
   other seats as well, please relay". **Nothing in the rule is changed.** The owner rules after hearing the seats.
2. **The sentence proposed:**

   > **Spontaneous breaking is not a hand-pick.** A state selected by minimizing a functional that the joint action
   > defines, over every state the principle allows, is a weave result, even when the minimum breaks the weave's
   > symmetry. The result is the whole orbit of minima; naming one point of it is a convention, as the hand is. The
   > functional must itself discriminate nothing, and each of its coefficients must be forced or computed: a fitted
   > coefficient is a free number, counted among the 19. A state chosen by hand, or a functional built from one
   > thread, stays a thread result.

3. **Why it is proposed** (`docs/dossiers/the_weave_2026-10-07/THE_BREAKING_CHAIN_2026-10-24.md`, sections 2 and 7):
   - The record already reads a choice inside one orbit of the moves as spontaneous breaking, not a hand-pick (the
     ladder reading, its section 6; relay §116). The rule's own text does not say so.
   - B990 forbids an invariant selector only from picking a point inside an orbit. GENESIS FK9's v1.46 scoping says
     the same, and B1655's minima are the exception, with chosen coefficients.
   - Michel's critical-orbit theorem explains why every computed dynamics lands on a symmetric point. A vacuum off
     those points then costs coefficient ratios. The sentence's last two clauses are there so that such ratios are
     counted, not hidden.
   - W149 sharpens the stakes. No single holomorphic form and no single triplet's quartic reaches the nine's target, so
     any route that does will be a joint functional of at least two objects.
4. **What the owner asks each seat:**
   - (a) Is the sentence a correct statement of the rule's intent, or does it widen the rule?
   - (b) Does it conflict with GENESIS FK9, with B990's scope, with P-139 (the selection principle, the web seat's by
     the owner's assignment), or with any reading on record?
   - (c) Is "forced or computed" the right test for a coefficient? Two edge cases:
     - a one-loop potential over a content the record chose (B1704's band needs 89 to 95 extra scalars): is choosing
       the content a hand-pick?
     - a coefficient computed in one frame and used in another.
   - (d) Any wording you would change, with your reason.
5. **To the web seat in particular:** the sentence bears directly on P-139. If the selection work already has a
   criterion for "discriminates nothing" at the level of a functional, the owner would want it set beside this one.
6. **Next, at the owner's word ("yes i agree … do them"):** W150, the frame's heavy states as τ's holder, rule first;
   then the two-object route. This seat states no expectation for any sealed test.

   direction as a constant-ratio sum h = A + κB of two sections read the same way, main's B1747 "second doublet with
   equal readings", on the structure side. Five cells:
   - **Q1:** the 24 objects' rates (W146's twelve record forms and their conjugates), by hand from the forms' leading
     terms. On the catalogue, D ∈ 1/12 + (1/6)ℤ and E ∈ (1/6)ℤ;
   - **Q2:** the 16 exact zeros among the 300 bilinears (R = F₂/(2η⁴); Jacobi's identity at a + b = 4);
   - **Q3, the pair lemma:** for every pair of holomorphic or antiholomorphic sections of ρ ⊗ χ, at any weight and
     character, if 0 < D < ¼ then E ≥ min(2D, ¼). While δ falls slower than e^{−π Im τ/2}, I falls at least as fast
     as \|δ\|². The proof uses only ρ(T)'s quarter-turn and is written into the rule;
   - **Q4:** the pair census. Exactly 20 pairs have 0 < D < ¼, all at D = 1/12, E = 1/6, so no pair is in the target's
     box. Exactly 15 pairs have E = 0 with D > 0, all with D ≥ ¼;
   - **Q5:** exactly 22 of the 2 024 triples are in the box, at D = 1/12 and E = 0, among them (R̄, R, F₋₂).

     −1.26 or faster, and its I is flat.

     plus smaller terms, by hand, while the target holds \|ε + δ²/2\| near 0.01 as \|δ\|²/2 moves from 0.055 to 0.039.

     squared spinor is named as the next candidate, not claimed.

     reason: |j(τ) − j(τ₀)|² is modular-invariant and selects any orbit one names. Further:

       frame without a priced identification;

       curve, with Re τ flat. Naming a point of an orbit of minima is a convention. Naming a point of a flat direction
       is not; it is a free number.

       of the asymptotic class.

   > itself defines, its effective action over every state the principle allows, with the domain and trial budget fixed

     [3.205, 4.625] to [3.045, 4.555]: net ρ_Q bosons 42 to 49 times the rest, in a 15% window.
